[^31-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–25 are 31:1–24 in most English Bibles.
[^31-2]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^31-3]: Verses 2–4 appear again, with small differences, in 71:1–3.
[^31-4]: Hebrew *ruach*: spirit, breath. Jesus speaks the first line of this verse on the cross in Luke 23:46.
[^31-5]: Some Hebrew manuscripts and the Septuagint read "You hate".
[^31-6]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love.
[^31-7]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self.
[^31-8]: Literally "my soul (*nephesh*) and my belly".
[^31-9]: The Septuagint reads "in poverty", from a Hebrew word that looks similar.
[^31-10]: The same phrase occurs in Jeremiah 6:25; 20:3, 10; 46:5; 49:29.
[^31-11]: Hebrew *Sheol*, the realm of the dead.
[^31-12]: Literally "from the plots of man". The Hebrew word for "plots" is found only here.
[^31-13]: Literally "from the strife of tongues".
[^31-14]: Or "a fortified city".
[^31-15]: Hebrew *chasidim*, "faithful, loyal ones", related to *chesed*, "faithful love".

## Chapter 32

*Status: draft*

**1** Of David. A maskil.[^32-1] Happy[^32-2] is the one whose offense is forgiven, whose sin is covered.[^32-3]

**2** Happy is the person to whom Yahweh[^32-4] does not count guilt, and in whose spirit there is no deceit.

**3** When I kept silent, my bones wasted away through my groaning[^32-5] all day long.

**4** For day and night your hand was heavy on me; my strength was dried up[^32-6] as in the heat of summer. Selah.[^32-7]

**5** I made my sin known to you, and I did not cover up my guilt. I said, "I will confess my offenses to Yahweh," and you forgave the guilt of my sin. Selah.

**6** So let everyone who is faithful[^32-8] pray to you while you may be found.[^32-9] Surely when the great waters overflow, they will not reach him.

**7** You are my hiding place; you protect me from trouble; you surround me with shouts of deliverance.[^32-10] Selah.

**8** I will instruct you and teach you in the way you should go; I will counsel you with my eye on you.[^32-11]

**9** Do not be like a horse or a mule, without understanding, which must be held in with bit and bridle, or it will not come near you.[^32-12]

**10** Many are the pains of the wicked, but the one who trusts in Yahweh is surrounded by faithful love.[^32-13]

**11** Rejoice in Yahweh and be glad, you righteous; shout for joy, all you upright in heart.

[^32-1]: *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". *Maskil*: a term of uncertain meaning, perhaps "a skillful psalm" or "a psalm of instruction". In the Hebrew text the title is part of verse 1. In this psalm it shares the verse with the first line, so the verse numbers match English Bibles.
[^32-2]: Hebrew *ashrei*: happy, fortunate; traditionally "blessed". Also in verse 2.
[^32-3]: Romans 4:7–8 quotes verses 1–2.
[^32-4]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^32-5]: Literally "my roaring".
[^32-6]: Literally "my sap was turned". Hebrew *leshad*, a rare word, perhaps "vitality, moisture".
[^32-7]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning. Also in verses 5 and 7.
[^32-8]: Hebrew *chasid*, "faithful, loyal", related to *chesed*, "faithful love".
[^32-9]: Literally "at a time of finding".
[^32-10]: The Hebrew of this phrase is unusual. Literally "shouts of escape".
[^32-11]: Here Yahweh seems to speak, though the speaker may be the psalmist teaching others.
[^32-12]: The Hebrew of this verse is difficult. Literally "with bit and bridle, its ornament, to curb it; not coming near to you".
[^32-13]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love.

## Chapter 33

*Status: draft*

**1** Shout for joy in Yahweh,[^33-1] you righteous! Praise is fitting for the upright.

**2** Give thanks to Yahweh with the lyre; make music to him with a ten-stringed harp.[^33-2]

**3** Sing to him a new song; play skillfully, with shouts of joy.

**4** For the word of Yahweh is upright, and all his work is done in faithfulness.

**5** He loves righteousness and justice; the earth is full of the faithful love[^33-3] of Yahweh.

**6** By the word of Yahweh the heavens were made, and all their host[^33-4] by the breath of his mouth.

**7** He gathers the waters of the sea as in a heap;[^33-5] he puts the deep waters in storehouses.

**8** Let all the earth fear Yahweh; let all who live in the world stand in awe of him.

**9** For he spoke, and it came to be; he commanded, and it stood firm.

**10** Yahweh frustrates the plans of the nations; he thwarts the purposes of the peoples.

**11** The plans of Yahweh stand forever, the purposes of his heart through all generations.

**12** Happy[^33-6] is the nation whose God is Yahweh, the people he has chosen as his inheritance.

**13** From heaven Yahweh looks down; he sees all human beings.

**14** From the place where he sits enthroned he watches all who live on the earth.

**15** He forms the hearts of them all; he understands all their deeds.

**16** A king is not saved by a great army; a warrior is not rescued by great strength.

**17** A horse is a false hope for victory;[^33-7] for all its great strength it cannot save.

**18** See, the eye of Yahweh is on those who fear him, on those who hope in his faithful love,

**19** to rescue them[^33-8] from death and keep them alive in famine.

**20** We wait[^33-9] for Yahweh; he is our help and our shield.

**21** For in him our heart rejoices, because we trust in his holy name.

**22** May your faithful love be on us, Yahweh, as we hope in you.

[^33-1]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^33-2]: Literally "with a harp of ten". The *kinnor* (lyre) and *nevel* (harp) were both stringed instruments.
[^33-3]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love.
[^33-4]: That is, the sun, moon and stars.
[^33-5]: The Septuagint reads "as in a wineskin", from the same consonants with different vowels.
[^33-6]: Hebrew *ashrei*: happy, fortunate; traditionally "blessed".
[^33-7]: Or "salvation". Literally "The horse is a lie for victory".
[^33-8]: Literally "their soul (*nephesh*)".
[^33-9]: Literally "Our soul (*nephesh*) waits".

## Chapter 34

*Status: draft*

**1** Of David, when he changed his behavior[^34-1] before Abimelech, who drove him out, and he went away.[^34-2]

**2** I will bless Yahweh[^34-3] at all times; his praise will always be in my mouth.[^34-4]

**3** My soul[^34-5] will boast in Yahweh; let the humble hear and be glad.

**4** Magnify Yahweh with me; let us exalt his name together.

**5** I sought Yahweh, and he answered me; he rescued me from all my fears.

**6** Those who looked to him were radiant, and their faces will never be ashamed.[^34-6]

**7** This afflicted man called, and Yahweh heard, and saved him from all his troubles.

**8** The angel of Yahweh camps around those who fear him, and rescues them.

**9** Taste and see that Yahweh is good.[^34-7] Happy[^34-8] is the one who takes refuge in him.

**10** Fear Yahweh, you his holy ones, for those who fear him lack nothing.

**11** Young lions[^34-9] may go hungry and starve, but those who seek Yahweh lack no good thing.

**12** Come, children, listen to me; I will teach you the fear of Yahweh.

**13** Who is the one who delights in life, and loves many days to see good?[^34-10]

**14** Keep your tongue from evil and your lips from speaking deceit.

**15** Turn away from evil and do good; seek peace and pursue it.

**16** The eyes of Yahweh are on the righteous, and his ears are turned to their cry for help.

**17** The face of Yahweh is against those who do evil, to cut off the memory of them from the earth.

**18** They cry out,[^34-11] and Yahweh hears, and rescues them from all their troubles.

**19** Yahweh is near to the brokenhearted, and saves those who are crushed in spirit.

**20** Many are the troubles of the righteous, but Yahweh rescues him from them all.

**21** He guards all his bones; not one of them will be broken.[^34-12]

**22** Evil will kill the wicked, and those who hate the righteous will be condemned.

**23** Yahweh redeems the life[^34-13] of his servants; none who take refuge in him will be condemned.

[^34-1]: Literally "changed his sense", that is, pretended to be insane. The story is told in 1 Samuel 21:11–16 (21:10–15 in English), where the king of Gath is called Achish. *Abimelech* may have been a title of Philistine kings (compare Genesis 20; 26).
[^34-2]: *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–23 are 34:1–22 in most English Bibles.
[^34-3]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^34-4]: Psalm 34 is an acrostic: in the Hebrew, each verse from verse 2 to verse 22 begins with the next letter of the alphabet. There is no verse for *vav*, and verse 23, beginning with *pe*, stands outside the alphabet as a closing line, as in Psalm 25.
[^34-5]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self.
[^34-6]: The Septuagint has commands: "Come to him and be enlightened, and your faces will never be put to shame."
[^34-7]: 1 Peter 2:3 echoes this line.
[^34-8]: Hebrew *ashrei*: happy, fortunate; traditionally "blessed".
[^34-9]: The Septuagint reads "The rich".
[^34-10]: 1 Peter 3:10–12 quotes verses 13–17.
[^34-11]: That is, the righteous of verse 16. The Septuagint reads "The righteous cry out".
[^34-12]: John 19:36 applies this verse, together with Exodus 12:46, to the death of Jesus.
[^34-13]: Hebrew *nephesh*.

## Chapter 35

*Status: draft*

**1** Of David.[^35-1] Contend, Yahweh,[^35-2] with those who contend with me; fight against those who fight against me.

**2** Take hold of shield and buckler,[^35-3] and rise up to help me.

**3** Draw the spear and the battle-axe[^35-4] against those who pursue me. Say to me,[^35-5] "I am your salvation."

**4** Let those who seek my life be put to shame and disgraced; let those who plan harm against me be turned back and confounded.

**5** Let them be like chaff before the wind, with the angel of Yahweh driving them away.

**6** Let their way be dark and slippery, with the angel of Yahweh pursuing them.

**7** For without cause they hid their net for me in a pit; without cause they dug it for my life.[^35-6]

**8** Let ruin come on him unawares; let the net he hid catch him; let him fall into it, to his ruin.

**9** Then my soul[^35-7] will rejoice in Yahweh and delight in his salvation.

**10** All my bones will say, "Yahweh, who is like you? You rescue the afflicted from those too strong for them, the afflicted and the needy from those who rob them."

**11** Malicious witnesses[^35-8] rise up; they question me about things I do not know.

**12** They repay me evil for good, leaving my soul bereaved.

**13** But as for me, when they were sick, my clothing was sackcloth; I humbled myself[^35-9] with fasting, and I prayed with my head bowed on my chest.[^35-10]

**14** I went about as though for my friend or my brother; I bowed down in mourning,[^35-11] like one who mourns for his mother.

**15** But when I stumbled, they rejoiced and gathered; attackers[^35-12] gathered against me, people I did not know; they tore at me without ceasing.

**16** Like godless people mocking at a feast,[^35-13] they gnashed their teeth at me.

**17** Lord, how long will you look on? Rescue my life[^35-14] from their ravages, my only life[^35-15] from the lions.

**18** I will give thanks to you in the great assembly; among a mighty throng I will praise you.

**19** Do not let those who are wrongfully my enemies gloat over me; do not let those who hate me without cause wink their eyes in malice.[^35-16]

**20** For they do not speak peace, but devise deceitful words against those who are quiet in the land.

**21** They open their mouths wide against me; they say, "Aha! Aha! Our eyes have seen it!"

**22** You have seen it, Yahweh; do not be silent. Lord, do not be far from me.

**23** Wake up! Rouse yourself to defend me, my God and my Lord, to plead my cause!

**24** Judge me[^35-17] according to your righteousness, Yahweh my God, and do not let them gloat over me.

**25** Do not let them say in their hearts, "Aha, just what we wanted!"[^35-18] Do not let them say, "We have swallowed him up."

**26** Let those who rejoice at my misfortune be put to shame and confounded together; let those who exalt themselves over me be clothed with shame and disgrace.

**27** Let those who delight in my vindication[^35-19] shout for joy and be glad; let them say always, "Great is Yahweh, who delights in the well-being[^35-20] of his servant!"

**28** Then my tongue will tell of your righteousness, of your praise all day long.

[^35-1]: *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is part of verse 1. In this psalm it shares the verse with the first line, so the verse numbers match English Bibles.
[^35-2]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^35-3]: Hebrew *magen* and *tsinnah*, a small round shield and a large one that covered the whole body.
[^35-4]: Hebrew *segor*, perhaps a weapon, or a verb: "and close the way".
[^35-5]: Literally "to my soul (*nephesh*)".
[^35-6]: Literally "For without cause they hid for me the pit of their net; without cause they dug for my soul (*nephesh*)".
[^35-7]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self.
[^35-8]: Literally "witnesses of violence".
[^35-9]: Literally "afflicted my soul (*nephesh*)", a phrase used of fasting (compare Leviticus 16:29).
[^35-10]: Literally "my prayer returned to my bosom". Or "may my prayer return to me", that is, unanswered.
[^35-11]: Or "in dark clothes", the clothing of mourning.
[^35-12]: Hebrew *nekhim*, a word of uncertain meaning, perhaps "attackers", "slanderers" or "outcasts".
[^35-13]: The Hebrew of this line is difficult. Literally "with the godless of the mockers of a cake".
[^35-14]: Hebrew *nephesh*.
[^35-15]: Literally "my only one", that is, my precious life (compare 22:21, 22:20 in English).
[^35-16]: John 15:25 quotes "They hated me without cause", from this verse or from 69:5 (69:4 in English).
[^35-17]: Or "Vindicate me".
[^35-18]: Literally "Aha, our soul (*nephesh*)!", that is, our desire.
[^35-19]: Or "my righteousness". Hebrew *tsedeq*.
[^35-20]: Hebrew *shalom*: peace, well-being.

## Chapter 36

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. Of the servant of Yahweh,[^36-1] of David.[^36-2]

**2** Rebellion speaks to the wicked deep in my heart:[^36-3] there is no fear of God before his eyes.[^36-4]

**3** For it flatters him in his own eyes, that his guilt will not be found out and hated.[^36-5]

**4** The words of his mouth are wickedness and deceit; he has stopped acting wisely and doing good.

**5** He plans wickedness on his bed; he sets himself on a path that is not good; he does not reject evil.

**6** Yahweh, your faithful love[^36-6] reaches to the heavens, your faithfulness to the clouds.

**7** Your righteousness is like the mighty mountains,[^36-7] your judgments like the great deep. Yahweh, you save humans and animals alike.

**8** How precious is your faithful love, God! Human beings take refuge in the shadow of your wings.

**9** They feast on the abundance of your house, and you give them drink from your river of delights.[^36-8]

**10** For with you is the fountain of life; in your light we see light.

**11** Continue your faithful love to those who know you, and your righteousness to the upright in heart.

**12** Do not let the foot of the proud come against me, or the hand of the wicked drive me away.

**13** There the evildoers lie fallen; they are thrust down and cannot rise.

[^36-1]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^36-2]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–13 are 36:1–12 in most English Bibles.
[^36-3]: The Hebrew of this line is difficult. Literally "An oracle of rebellion to the wicked in the middle of my heart". *Oracle* (*ne'um*) is the word used in "says Yahweh". Or "I know deep in my heart what rebellion says to the wicked". The Septuagint reads "The lawless one says in himself that he will sin".
[^36-4]: Romans 3:18 quotes the second line of this verse.
[^36-5]: The Hebrew of this verse is difficult. Literally "For he makes it smooth to him in his eyes, to find his guilt, to hate".
[^36-6]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love.
[^36-7]: Literally "the mountains of God".
[^36-8]: The word for "delights" (*adanim*) is related to the name *Eden*.

## Chapter 37

*Status: draft*

**1** Of David.[^37-1] Do not fret because of evildoers; do not envy those who do wrong.[^37-2]

**2** For like grass they will soon wither, and like green plants they will fade.

**3** Trust in Yahweh[^37-3] and do good; live in the land and cultivate faithfulness.[^37-4]

**4** Delight yourself in Yahweh, and he will give you the desires of your heart.

**5** Commit your way to Yahweh;[^37-5] trust in him, and he will act.

**6** He will make your righteousness shine like the light, and your justice like the noonday sun.

**7** Be still before Yahweh and wait patiently for him. Do not fret because of the one who prospers in his way, the one who carries out evil schemes.

**8** Stop being angry and abandon your rage; do not fret: it leads only to evil.

**9** For evildoers will be cut off, but those who wait for Yahweh, they will inherit the land.

**10** Yet a little while and the wicked will be no more; you will look closely at his place, and he will not be there.

**11** But the humble will inherit the land[^37-6] and delight in abundant peace.

**12** The wicked plots against the righteous and gnashes his teeth at him.

**13** The Lord laughs at him, for he sees that his day is coming.

**14** The wicked draw the sword and bend their bow to bring down the afflicted and the needy, to slaughter those whose way is upright.

**15** Their sword will enter their own heart, and their bows will be broken.

**16** Better is the little that the righteous has than the wealth of many wicked.

**17** For the arms of the wicked will be broken, but Yahweh upholds the righteous.

**18** Yahweh knows the days of the blameless, and their inheritance will last forever.

**19** They will not be put to shame in evil times; in days of famine they will have plenty.

**20** But the wicked will perish; the enemies of Yahweh are like the glory of the pastures:[^37-7] they vanish; like smoke they vanish.

**21** The wicked borrows and does not repay, but the righteous is generous and gives.

**22** For those whom he blesses will inherit the land, but those whom he curses will be cut off.

**23** A man's steps are made firm by Yahweh, and he delights in his way.[^37-8]

**24** Though he falls, he will not be thrown down, for Yahweh holds his hand.

**25** I have been young and now I am old, yet I have not seen the righteous abandoned, or his children begging for bread.

**26** He is always generous and lends, and his children become a blessing.

**27** Turn away from evil and do good, and you will live in the land forever.

**28** For Yahweh loves justice and will not abandon his faithful ones.[^37-9] They will be kept safe forever, but the children of the wicked will be cut off.

**29** The righteous will inherit the land and live in it forever.

**30** The mouth of the righteous utters wisdom, and his tongue speaks justice.

**31** The law[^37-10] of his God is in his heart; his steps do not slip.

**32** The wicked watches for the righteous and seeks to kill him.

**33** Yahweh will not abandon him to his power, or let him be condemned when he is brought to trial.

**34** Wait for Yahweh and keep his way, and he will exalt you to inherit the land; you will see it when the wicked are cut off.

**35** I have seen a wicked and ruthless man, spreading himself like a luxuriant tree in its native soil.[^37-11]

**36** But he passed away,[^37-12] and look, he was gone; I searched for him, but he could not be found.

**37** Watch the blameless and observe the upright, for there is a future[^37-13] for the person of peace.

**38** But rebels will be destroyed altogether; the future of the wicked will be cut off.

**39** The salvation of the righteous comes from Yahweh; he is their stronghold in time of trouble.

**40** Yahweh helps them and rescues them; he rescues them from the wicked and saves them, because they take refuge in him.

[^37-1]: *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is part of verse 1. In this psalm it shares the verse with the first line, so the verse numbers match English Bibles.
[^37-2]: Psalm 37 is an acrostic: in the Hebrew, each letter of the alphabet in turn begins a section, usually of two verses (*aleph* at verse 1, *bet* at verse 3, and so on, to *tav* at verse 39). The *ayin* section begins in the middle of verse 28, with *le'olam*, "forever".
[^37-3]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^37-4]: Or "and graze in safety", "and feed on faithfulness".
[^37-5]: Literally "Roll your way on Yahweh".
[^37-6]: Or "the earth". Jesus echoes this line in Matthew 5:5.
[^37-7]: Or "like the fat of lambs", which is burned up in sacrifice.
[^37-8]: It is not clear whether Yahweh delights in the man's way, or the man in Yahweh's.
[^37-9]: Hebrew *chasidim*, "faithful, loyal ones", related to *chesed*, "faithful love".
[^37-10]: Hebrew *torah*, "instruction, teaching".
[^37-11]: Literally "like a green native". The Septuagint reads "like the cedars of Lebanon".
[^37-12]: The Septuagint reads "I passed by".
[^37-13]: Or "descendants". Also in verse 38.

## Chapter 38

*Status: draft*

**1** A psalm of David.[^38-1] To bring to remembrance.[^38-2]

**2** Yahweh,[^38-3] do not rebuke me in your anger, or discipline me in your wrath.[^38-4]

**3** For your arrows have sunk into me, and your hand has come down on me.

**4** There is no soundness in my flesh because of your indignation; there is no health[^38-5] in my bones because of my sin.

**5** For my guilt has gone over my head; like a heavy burden it is too heavy for me.

**6** My wounds stink and fester because of my foolishness.

**7** I am bent over and bowed down greatly; all day long I go about in mourning.

**8** For my sides[^38-6] are filled with burning, and there is no soundness in my flesh.

**9** I am numb and badly crushed; I groan[^38-7] because of the turmoil of my heart.

**10** Lord, all my longing is before you; my sighing is not hidden from you.

**11** My heart pounds, my strength fails me; even the light of my eyes has gone from me.

**12** My friends and companions stand aloof from my affliction;[^38-8] my neighbors stand far off.

**13** Those who seek my life[^38-9] lay snares; those who seek my harm speak of ruin and plot deceit all day long.

**14** But I am like a deaf man who does not hear, like a mute man who does not open his mouth.

**15** I have become like a man who does not hear, in whose mouth there are no arguments.

**16** For in you, Yahweh, I hope; you will answer, Lord my God.

**17** For I said, "Do not let them gloat over me, who exalt themselves over me when my foot slips."

**18** For I am ready to fall, and my pain is always before me.

**19** I confess my guilt; I am troubled by my sin.

**20** My enemies are full of life and strong;[^38-10] many are those who hate me wrongfully.

**21** Those who repay evil for good accuse me[^38-11] because I pursue good.

**22** Do not abandon me, Yahweh; my God, do not be far from me.

**23** Come quickly to help me, Lord, my salvation.

[^38-1]: *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–23 are 38:1–22 in most English Bibles.
[^38-2]: Hebrew *lehazkir*, "to cause to remember" or "to make a memorial". The psalm may have been sung with the memorial portion of an offering (Leviticus 2:2), or it may call on God to remember the one who prays. Also in the title of Psalm 70.
[^38-3]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^38-4]: This verse is almost the same as 6:2 (6:1 in English).
[^38-5]: Hebrew *shalom*: peace, well-being, wholeness.
[^38-6]: Literally "my loins".
[^38-7]: Literally "I roar".
[^38-8]: Or "my disease". Hebrew *nega*, "a blow, a plague".
[^38-9]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self.
[^38-10]: Literally "My enemies are living, they are strong".
[^38-11]: Hebrew *yistenuni*, from the same root as *satan*, "adversary, accuser".

## Chapter 39

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. For Jeduthun.[^39-1] A psalm of David.[^39-2]

**2** I said, "I will guard my ways, so that I do not sin with my tongue; I will keep a muzzle on my mouth as long as the wicked are in my presence."

**3** I was silent and still; I kept quiet, even from good,[^39-3] and my pain grew worse.

**4** My heart grew hot within me; as I mused, a fire burned. Then I spoke with my tongue:

**5** "Yahweh,[^39-4] let me know my end, and what is the measure of my days; let me know how fleeting I am.

**6** See, you have made my days a few handbreadths,[^39-5] and my lifetime is as nothing before you. Surely every human being, however firmly he stands, is only a breath.[^39-6] Selah.[^39-7]

**7** Surely everyone goes about like a shadow;[^39-8] surely they are in turmoil for nothing. He heaps up wealth and does not know who will gather it.

**8** And now, Lord, what do I wait for? My hope is in you.

**9** Rescue me from all my offenses; do not make me the scorn of fools.

**10** I am silent; I do not open my mouth, for it is you who have done this.

**11** Remove your blow from me; I am worn out by the hostility of your hand.

**12** When you discipline someone with rebukes for guilt, you consume like a moth what is dear to him. Surely every human being is only a breath. Selah.

**13** Hear my prayer, Yahweh, and listen to my cry for help; do not be silent at my tears. For I am a foreigner with you, a temporary resident,[^39-9] like all my ancestors.

**14** Look away from me, so that I may smile again, before I go away and am no more."

[^39-1]: One of David's chief musicians (1 Chronicles 16:41; 25:1). The Hebrew text is written "Jedithun" but read "Jeduthun".
[^39-2]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–14 are 39:1–13 in most English Bibles.
[^39-3]: Or "to no avail".
[^39-4]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^39-5]: A handbreadth was about 3 inches (7.5 cm), the width of four fingers.
[^39-6]: Hebrew *hevel*, "breath, vapor", the word used throughout Ecclesiastes ("Vapor of vapors", Ecclesiastes 1:2). Also in verses 7 and 12.
[^39-7]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning. Also in verse 12.
[^39-8]: Literally "as an image".
[^39-9]: Hebrew *ger* and *toshav*, a foreigner living in the land and a temporary resident, without the rights of a citizen (compare Leviticus 25:23; 1 Chronicles 29:15).

## Chapter 40

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. Of David. A psalm.[^40-1]

**2** I waited patiently for Yahweh;[^40-2] he turned to me and heard my cry for help.

**3** He drew me up from the pit of destruction,[^40-3] out of the miry clay; he set my feet on a rock and made my steps secure.

**4** He put a new song in my mouth, a song of praise to our God. Many will see and fear,[^40-4] and will trust in Yahweh.

**5** Happy[^40-5] is the one who makes Yahweh his trust, who does not turn to the proud, or to those who go astray after lies.[^40-6]

**6** Many are the wonders you have done, Yahweh my God, and your plans for us; no one can compare with you.[^40-7] If I would proclaim them and speak of them, they are too many to count.

**7** Sacrifice and offering you did not desire, but you have opened my ears;[^40-8] burnt offering and sin offering[^40-9] you did not ask for.

**8** Then I said, "Here I am, I have come; in the scroll of the book it is written about me.[^40-10]

**9** I delight to do your will, my God; your law[^40-11] is within my heart."[^40-12]

**10** I have proclaimed the good news[^40-13] of righteousness in the great assembly. See, I do not hold back my lips, Yahweh, as you know.

**11** I have not hidden your righteousness within my heart; I have spoken of your faithfulness and your salvation. I have not concealed your faithful love and your truth[^40-14] from the great assembly.

**12** You, Yahweh, will not withhold your compassion from me; your faithful love and your truth will always protect me.

**13** For evils without number have surrounded me; my sins have overtaken me, and I cannot see. They are more than the hairs of my head, and my heart fails me.

**14** Be pleased, Yahweh, to rescue me; Yahweh, come quickly to help me.[^40-15]

**15** Let those who seek my life,[^40-16] to sweep it away, be put to shame and confounded together; let those who delight in my harm be turned back and disgraced.

**16** Let those who say to me, "Aha! Aha!" be appalled because of their shame.

**17** But let all who seek you rejoice and be glad in you; let those who love your salvation say always, "Great is Yahweh!"

**18** As for me, I am afflicted and needy; may the Lord think of me. You are my help and my deliverer; my God, do not delay.

[^40-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–18 are 40:1–17 in most English Bibles.
[^40-2]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles. *I waited patiently*: literally "I waited and waited".
[^40-3]: Literally "the pit of roaring", perhaps of rushing water, or "of desolation".
[^40-4]: In Hebrew "see" (*yir'u*) and "fear" (*yira'u*) sound alike.
[^40-5]: Hebrew *ashrei*: happy, fortunate; traditionally "blessed".
[^40-6]: Or "to false gods". Hebrew *rehavim*, "the proud", and *satei khazav*, "those who turn aside to falsehood", may both refer to idols and those who worship them.
[^40-7]: Or "they cannot be set out in order before you".
[^40-8]: Literally "ears you have dug for me", that is, you have made me able to hear and obey. Hebrews 10:5–7 quotes verses 7–9 from the Septuagint, which reads "but a body you have prepared for me".
[^40-9]: Hebrew *chata'ah*, a form of *chatta't*. Or "purification offering".
[^40-10]: Or "it is written for me", that is, prescribed for me.
[^40-11]: Hebrew *torah*, "instruction, teaching".
[^40-12]: Literally "in the middle of my inner parts".
[^40-13]: Hebrew *bissarti*, "I brought good news". In the Septuagint this is *euangelizomai*, the verb behind "gospel".
[^40-14]: Hebrew *chesed ve'emet*: loyal, faithful love and reliability, truth (compare 25:10). Also in verse 12.
[^40-15]: Verses 14–18 appear again, with small differences, as Psalm 70:2–6 (70:1–5 in English).
[^40-16]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self.

## Chapter 41

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. A psalm of David.[^41-1]

**2** Happy[^41-2] is the one who considers the poor; in the day of trouble Yahweh[^41-3] will rescue him.

**3** Yahweh will protect him and keep him alive; he will be called happy in the land. Do not hand him over to the will[^41-4] of his enemies!

**4** Yahweh will sustain him on his sickbed; in his illness you restore him to health.[^41-5]

**5** As for me, I said, "Yahweh, be gracious to me; heal me,[^41-6] for I have sinned against you."

**6** My enemies speak evil of me: "When will he die, and his name perish?"

**7** When one of them comes to see me, he speaks empty words, while his heart gathers up mischief; he goes out and tells it abroad.

**8** All who hate me whisper together against me; they imagine the worst for me:

**9** "A deadly thing[^41-7] has been poured into him; now that he lies down, he will not rise again."

**10** Even my close friend,[^41-8] whom I trusted, who ate my bread, has lifted up his heel against me.[^41-9]

**11** But you, Yahweh, be gracious to me and raise me up, so that I may repay them.

**12** By this I know that you delight in me: that my enemy does not shout in triumph over me.

**13** As for me, you uphold me in my integrity, and set me in your presence forever.

**14** Blessed be Yahweh, the God of Israel, from everlasting to everlasting. Amen and Amen.[^41-10]

[^41-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–14 are 41:1–13 in most English Bibles.
[^41-2]: Hebrew *ashrei*: happy, fortunate; traditionally "blessed".
[^41-3]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^41-4]: Literally "the soul (*nephesh*)", that is, the desire.
[^41-5]: Literally "all his bed you have turned in his sickness".
[^41-6]: Literally "heal my soul (*nephesh*)".
[^41-7]: Literally "a thing of *beliyya'al*", "worthlessness", here perhaps a deadly disease or a curse (compare 18:5, 18:4 in English).
[^41-8]: Literally "the man of my peace".
[^41-9]: That is, has turned against me. John 13:18 quotes this line.
[^41-10]: This blessing closes Book 1 of the Psalms (Psalms 1–41). Each of the next three books ends in a similar way (72:18–19; 89:53, 89:52 in English; 106:48); compare 1 Chronicles 16:36.

## Chapter 42

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. A maskil of the sons of Korah.[^42-1]

**2** As a deer[^42-2] longs for streams of water, so my soul[^42-3] longs for you, God.[^42-4]

**3** My soul thirsts for God, for the living God. When may I come and appear before God?[^42-5]

**4** My tears have been my food day and night, while people say to me all day long, "Where is your God?"

**5** These things I remember as I pour out my soul:[^42-6] how I used to go along with the throng,[^42-7] leading them in procession[^42-8] to the house of God, with shouts of joy and thanksgiving, a crowd keeping festival.

**6** Why are you downcast, my soul? Why are you in turmoil within me? Hope in God, for I will yet praise him for the saving help of his presence.[^42-9]

**7** My God, my soul is downcast within me; so I remember you from the land of the Jordan and the heights of Hermon,[^42-10] from Mount Mizar.

**8** Deep calls to deep at the roar of your waterfalls;[^42-11] all your breakers and your waves have swept over me.

**9** By day Yahweh[^42-12] commands his faithful love,[^42-13] and at night his song[^42-14] is with me, a prayer to the God of my life.

**10** I say to God, my rock, "Why have you forgotten me? Why must I go about in mourning,[^42-15] oppressed by the enemy?"

**11** As if my bones were being crushed,[^42-16] my adversaries taunt me, saying to me all day long, "Where is your God?"

**12** Why are you downcast, my soul? Why are you in turmoil within me? Hope in God, for I will yet praise him, my saving help[^42-17] and my God.

[^42-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Maskil*: a term of uncertain meaning, perhaps "a skillful psalm" or "a psalm of instruction". *Of the sons of Korah*: or "for the sons of Korah", a family of Levites who served as temple singers (2 Chronicles 20:19). In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–12 are 42:1–11 in most English Bibles. Psalms 42 and 43 were probably once a single psalm: they share a refrain (42:6, 12; 43:5), Psalm 43 has no title of its own, and many Hebrew manuscripts write them as one.
[^42-2]: The noun is masculine but the verb is feminine, so perhaps "a doe".
[^42-3]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self. Also in verses 3, 5, 6, 7 and 12.
[^42-4]: Psalms 42–83 are often called the "Elohistic Psalter". In them God is mostly called *Elohim*, "God", where other psalms use his personal name, Yahweh. Compare Psalm 53 with Psalm 14, and Psalm 70 with 40:14–18 (40:13–17 in most English Bibles), where the same lines appear with *Elohim* in place of Yahweh. This translation renders each name as it stands.
[^42-5]: The vowel points read "appear before God", the usual phrase for coming to worship at the sanctuary (Exodus 23:15; 34:23). The same consonants can be read "see the face of God".
[^42-6]: Literally "I remember these things, and I pour out my soul upon me", that is, in grief.
[^42-7]: Hebrew *sakh*, found only here, so the meaning is uncertain.
[^42-8]: Hebrew *eddaddem*, an uncertain form, perhaps "I walked slowly with them".
[^42-9]: Literally "the salvations of his face". In verse 12 and 43:5 the refrain ends "the salvation of my face and my God". The Septuagint has that wording here too, joining the first word of verse 7, "my God", to this verse.
[^42-10]: Literally "the Hermons", the peaks of Mount Hermon near the sources of the Jordan in the far north. *Mizar* means "small"; the mountain is unknown.
[^42-11]: Or "channels", "torrents". The word appears elsewhere only in 2 Samuel 5:8.
[^42-12]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^42-13]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love.
[^42-14]: The Hebrew text is written "a song" but read "his song".
[^42-15]: Literally "dark", wearing the dark clothes of mourning.
[^42-16]: Literally "With a shattering in my bones".
[^42-17]: Literally "the salvation of my face".

## Chapter 43

*Status: draft*

**1** Vindicate me, God,[^43-1] and plead my case against an unfaithful nation;[^43-2] rescue me from the deceitful and unjust.[^43-3]

**2** For you are God my stronghold. Why have you rejected me? Why must I go about in mourning,[^43-4] oppressed by the enemy?

**3** Send out your light and your truth; let them lead me. Let them bring me to your holy mountain, to the place where you dwell.[^43-5]

**4** Then I will go to the altar of God, to God, my joy and my delight,[^43-6] and I will praise you with the lyre, God, my God.

**5** Why are you downcast, my soul?[^43-7] Why are you in turmoil within me? Hope in God, for I will yet praise him, my saving help[^43-8] and my God.

[^43-1]: Psalm 43 has no title, and its refrain (verse 5) is the refrain of Psalm 42 (42:6, 12). The two were probably once a single psalm.
[^43-2]: Hebrew *lo-chasid*, "not faithful", that is, ungodly. *Chasid* is related to *chesed*, "faithful love".
[^43-3]: Literally "from a man of deceit and injustice".
[^43-4]: Literally "dark", wearing the dark clothes of mourning.
[^43-5]: Literally "to your dwellings".
[^43-6]: Literally "the joy of my rejoicing".
[^43-7]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self.
[^43-8]: Literally "the salvation of my face".

## Chapter 44

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. Of the sons of Korah. A maskil.[^44-1]

**2** God, we have heard with our own ears; our ancestors have told us the work you did in their days, in the days of old.

**3** With your own hand you drove out nations, but them you planted; you brought disaster on peoples, but them you set free.[^44-2]

**4** For it was not by their sword that they took the land, and their own arm did not give them victory; it was your right hand and your arm and the light of your face, because you delighted in them.

**5** You are my King, God; command victories[^44-3] for Jacob.

**6** Through you we gore our adversaries; through your name we trample those who rise up against us.

**7** For I do not trust in my bow, and my sword does not save me.

**8** But you have saved us from our adversaries, and you have put those who hate us to shame.

**9** In God we have boasted all day long, and we will praise your name forever. Selah.[^44-4]

**10** Yet you have rejected us and humiliated us; you no longer go out with our armies.

**11** You make us turn back from the adversary, and those who hate us take plunder for themselves.

**12** You hand us over like sheep to be eaten, and you have scattered us among the nations.

**13** You sell your people for nothing; you make no profit from their price.

**14** You make us a disgrace to our neighbors, the scorn and mockery of those around us.

**15** You make us a byword among the nations; the peoples shake their heads at us.

**16** All day long my disgrace is before me, and the shame of my face has covered me,

**17** at the voice of the one who taunts and reviles, at the sight of the enemy and the avenger.

**18** All this has come upon us, though we have not forgotten you or been false to your covenant.

**19** Our hearts have not turned back, and our steps have not strayed from your path.

**20** Yet you have crushed us in the haunt of jackals, and covered us with deep darkness.[^44-5]

**21** If we had forgotten the name of our God, or spread out our hands to a foreign god,

**22** would not God have searched this out? For he knows the secrets of the heart.

**23** Yet for your sake we are killed all day long; we are counted as sheep for the slaughter.[^44-6]

**24** Wake up! Why do you sleep, Lord? Rouse yourself! Do not reject us forever.

**25** Why do you hide your face, and forget our affliction and our oppression?

**26** For we have sunk down to the dust; our bodies cling to the ground.[^44-7]

**27** Rise up and help us! Redeem us for the sake of your faithful love.[^44-8]

[^44-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of the sons of Korah*: or "for the sons of Korah", a family of Levites who served as temple singers. *Maskil*: a term of uncertain meaning, perhaps "a skillful psalm" or "a psalm of instruction". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–27 are 44:1–26 in most English Bibles.
[^44-2]: *Them*: our ancestors (verse 2). *Set free*: or "let spread out", like a vine sending out its branches (compare 80:12, English 80:11).
[^44-3]: Literally "salvations".
[^44-4]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning.
[^44-5]: Hebrew *tsalmavet*, "deep darkness", traditionally read as "the shadow of death".
[^44-6]: Romans 8:36 quotes this verse.
[^44-7]: Literally "For our soul (*nephesh*) is bowed down to the dust; our belly clings to the ground."
[^44-8]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love.

## Chapter 45

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. According to "Lilies".[^45-1] Of the sons of Korah. A maskil. A love song.[^45-2]

**2** My heart overflows with a good theme;[^45-3] I speak my verses for the king;[^45-4] my tongue is the pen of a skillful scribe.

**3** You are the most handsome of all human beings; grace has been poured on your lips; so God has blessed you forever.

**4** Strap your sword on your thigh, mighty warrior, in your splendor and your majesty.

**5** In your majesty ride on to victory[^45-5] for the cause of truth and humility and righteousness;[^45-6] let your right hand teach you awesome deeds.

**6** Your arrows are sharp in the hearts of the king's enemies; peoples fall beneath you.[^45-7]

**7** Your throne, God, is forever and ever;[^45-8] the scepter of your kingdom is a scepter of justice.[^45-9]

**8** You love righteousness and hate wickedness; so God, your God, has anointed you with the oil of gladness above your companions.[^45-10]

**9** All your robes are fragrant with myrrh and aloes and cassia; from ivory palaces the music of strings[^45-11] makes you glad.

**10** Daughters of kings are among your honored women; at your right hand stands the queen[^45-12] in gold of Ophir.[^45-13]

**11** Listen, daughter, consider and turn your ear: forget your people and your father's house.

**12** Then the king will desire your beauty. He is your lord, so bow down to him.

**13** The daughter of Tyre[^45-14] comes with a gift; the richest of the people seek your favor.

**14** The king's daughter is all glorious within;[^45-15] her clothing is woven with gold.

**15** In embroidered robes she is led to the king; the young women, her companions, follow her; they are brought to you.

**16** They are led in with gladness and rejoicing; they enter the palace of the king.

**17** Your sons will take the place of your ancestors; you will make them princes throughout the land.[^45-16]

**18** I will make your name remembered in every generation; so the peoples will praise you forever and ever.

[^45-1]: Hebrew *al-shoshannim*, "according to lilies", probably the name of a tune.
[^45-2]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of the sons of Korah*: or "for the sons of Korah", a family of Levites who served as temple singers. *Maskil*: a term of uncertain meaning, perhaps "a skillful psalm" or "a psalm of instruction". *A love song*: literally "a song of loves", perhaps "a wedding song". The psalm celebrates a king's wedding. In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–18 are 45:1–17 in most English Bibles.
[^45-3]: Literally "a good word".
[^45-4]: Literally "I say my works to the king".
[^45-5]: Literally "succeed, ride". The verse begins "And your majesty", repeating the last word of verse 4.
[^45-6]: Literally "for the word of truth, and humility of righteousness". The Hebrew is compressed.
[^45-7]: The Hebrew order is "Your arrows are sharp, peoples fall beneath you, in the heart of the king's enemies".
[^45-8]: The Hebrew *kis'akha elohim* most naturally means "Your throne, God", addressing the king as *elohim*, "God". No other Israelite king is called this, though compare Isaiah 9:5 (9:6 in most English Bibles), where the royal child is called "Mighty God" (*el gibbor*). Other possible readings are "Your throne is God's throne forever" (compare 1 Chronicles 29:23, "Solomon sat on the throne of Yahweh as king") and "Your throne is like God's, forever". Verse 8 distinguishes the king from "God, your God". The Septuagint reads "Your throne, O God, is forever and ever", and Hebrews 1:8–9 quotes verses 7–8 in that form, applying them to the Son.
[^45-9]: Or "of uprightness".
[^45-10]: Or "so, God, your God has anointed you", continuing to address the king. Hebrew *meshachakha*, "has anointed you", from the same root as *mashiach*, "anointed one".
[^45-11]: Hebrew *minni*, probably "strings" (compare 150:4).
[^45-12]: Hebrew *shegal*, the king's consort.
[^45-13]: A land famous for its fine gold (1 Kings 9:28).
[^45-14]: That is, the people of Tyre, or perhaps a princess of Tyre.
[^45-15]: That is, inside the palace, or in her chamber.
[^45-16]: Or "in all the earth".

## Chapter 46

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. Of the sons of Korah. According to Alamoth.[^46-1] A song.[^46-2]

**2** God is our refuge and strength, a help in trouble, always ready to be found.[^46-3]

**3** So we will not fear, though the earth gives way, though the mountains topple into the heart of the seas,

**4** though its waters roar and foam, and the mountains quake at its surging. Selah.[^46-4]

**5** There is a river whose streams make glad the city of God, the holy dwelling place of the Most High.[^46-5]

**6** God is in her midst; she will not be shaken. God will help her at the break of day.[^46-6]

**7** Nations roar, kingdoms totter; he raises his voice, and the earth melts.

**8** Yahweh of Armies[^46-7] is with us; the God of Jacob is our fortress. Selah.

**9** Come, see the works of Yahweh, the desolations[^46-8] he has brought on the earth.

**10** He makes wars cease to the end of the earth; he breaks the bow and snaps the spear; he burns the chariots[^46-9] with fire.

**11** "Be still,[^46-10] and know that I am God. I will be exalted among the nations; I will be exalted in the earth."

**12** Yahweh of Armies is with us; the God of Jacob is our fortress. Selah.

[^46-1]: Hebrew *al-alamot*, a musical term of uncertain meaning, perhaps "for soprano voices" (compare 1 Chronicles 15:20).
[^46-2]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of the sons of Korah*: or "for the sons of Korah", a family of Levites who served as temple singers. In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–12 are 46:1–11 in most English Bibles.
[^46-3]: Literally "a help in troubles, found very much".
[^46-4]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning. Also in verses 8 and 12.
[^46-5]: Hebrew *Elyon*, a title of God. Literally "the holy place of the dwellings of the Most High".
[^46-6]: Literally "at the turning of the morning".
[^46-7]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles. *Yahweh of Armies* (Hebrew *YHWH tseva'ot*) is traditionally "the LORD of hosts" or "the LORD Almighty". *Tseva'ot* means armies: the heavenly hosts and the armies of Israel. Also in verse 12.
[^46-8]: Or "the astonishing deeds".
[^46-9]: Hebrew *agalot*, "carts, wagons". The Septuagint reads "shields".
[^46-10]: Or "Stop!", "Let go!"

## Chapter 47

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. Of the sons of Korah. A psalm.[^47-1]

**2** Clap your hands, all you peoples; shout to God with loud songs of joy.

**3** For Yahweh,[^47-2] the Most High,[^47-3] is awesome, a great King over all the earth.

**4** He subdues peoples under us, and nations under our feet.

**5** He chose our inheritance for us, the pride of Jacob, whom he loves. Selah.[^47-4]

**6** God has gone up with a shout, Yahweh with the sound of the ram's horn.[^47-5]

**7** Sing praises to God, sing praises; sing praises to our King, sing praises.

**8** For God is the King of all the earth; sing praises with a skillful psalm.[^47-6]

**9** God reigns over the nations; God sits on his holy throne.

**10** The nobles of the peoples have gathered as the people of the God of Abraham.[^47-7] For the shields of the earth[^47-8] belong to God; he is highly exalted.

[^47-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of the sons of Korah*: or "for the sons of Korah", a family of Levites who served as temple singers. In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–10 are 47:1–9 in most English Bibles.
[^47-2]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^47-3]: Hebrew *Elyon*, a title of God.
[^47-4]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning.
[^47-5]: Hebrew *shofar*.
[^47-6]: Hebrew *maskil*, the term found in the titles of some psalms (see 42:1).
[^47-7]: The Septuagint reads "with the God of Abraham".
[^47-8]: That is, the rulers who protect the earth's peoples.

## Chapter 48

*Status: draft*

**1** A song. A psalm of the sons of Korah.[^48-1]

**2** Great is Yahweh,[^48-2] and most worthy of praise, in the city of our God, his holy mountain.

**3** Beautiful in its height, the joy of all the earth, is Mount Zion, the heights of Zaphon,[^48-3] the city of the great King.

**4** God, in her citadels, has shown himself a fortress.

**5** For see, the kings assembled; they advanced together.

**6** They saw, and they were astounded; they were terrified and fled in haste.

**7** Trembling seized them there, pain like a woman in labor.

**8** With an east wind you shatter the ships of Tarshish.[^48-4]

**9** As we have heard, so we have seen, in the city of Yahweh of Armies,[^48-5] in the city of our God: God will establish it forever. Selah.[^48-6]

**10** We ponder your faithful love,[^48-7] God, within your temple.

**11** Like your name, God, your praise reaches to the ends of the earth; your right hand is full of righteousness.

**12** Let Mount Zion rejoice; let the daughters of Judah[^48-8] be glad because of your judgments.

**13** Walk around Zion, go all around her; count her towers.

**14** Consider well her ramparts; pass through her citadels,[^48-9] so that you may tell the next generation

**15** that this is God, our God, forever and ever. He will guide us until death.[^48-10]

[^48-1]: *Of the sons of Korah*: or "for the sons of Korah", a family of Levites who served as temple singers. In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–15 are 48:1–14 in most English Bibles.
[^48-2]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^48-3]: Or "in the far north". *Tsaphon*, "north", was also the name of a mountain in Syria where Canaanite myth placed the home of the gods (compare Isaiah 14:13).
[^48-4]: Large seagoing ships.
[^48-5]: Hebrew *YHWH tseva'ot*, traditionally "the LORD of hosts" or "the LORD Almighty". *Tseva'ot* means armies: the heavenly hosts and the armies of Israel.
[^48-6]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning.
[^48-7]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love.
[^48-8]: That is, the towns of Judah.
[^48-9]: Hebrew *passegu*, found only here; the meaning is uncertain, perhaps "view".
[^48-10]: Hebrew *al-mut*, "until death" or "over death". The Septuagint reads "forever". Some scholars take the words as a musical direction, like *almut labben* in the title of Psalm 9.

## Chapter 49

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. Of the sons of Korah. A psalm.[^49-1]

**2** Hear this, all you peoples; listen, all who live in the world,

**3** both low and high,[^49-2] rich and poor together.

**4** My mouth will speak wisdom, and the meditation of my heart will be understanding.

**5** I will turn my ear to a proverb;[^49-3] I will set out my riddle to the music of the lyre.

**6** Why should I be afraid in days of trouble, when the wickedness of those who dog my steps[^49-4] surrounds me,

**7** those who trust in their wealth and boast of their great riches?

**8** No one can ever ransom a brother,[^49-5] or pay God the price for his life.

**9** For the ransom of their life[^49-6] is costly, and it will always fall short,[^49-7]

**10** that one should live on forever and never see the pit.[^49-8]

**11** For one sees that the wise die; the fool and the senseless perish together, and leave their wealth to others.

**12** Their inner thought is that their houses will last forever,[^49-9] their dwellings for all generations; they have named lands after themselves.

**13** But a human being in his splendor does not endure;[^49-10] he is like the animals that perish.

**14** This is the way of those who are foolishly confident, and of those after them who delight in their words.[^49-11] Selah.[^49-12]

**15** Like sheep they are appointed for Sheol;[^49-13] death will be their shepherd. The upright will rule over them in the morning, and their form[^49-14] will waste away in Sheol, far from its lofty dwelling.[^49-15]

**16** But God will redeem my life from the power of Sheol,[^49-16] for he will take me.[^49-17] Selah.

**17** Do not be afraid when someone grows rich, when the glory of his house increases;

**18** for when he dies he will take nothing with him; his glory will not go down after him.

**19** Though in his lifetime he congratulates himself,[^49-18] and people praise you when you do well for yourself,

**20** he will go to the generation of his ancestors,[^49-19] who will never see the light.

**21** A human being in his splendor, without understanding, is like the animals that perish.[^49-20]

[^49-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of the sons of Korah*: or "for the sons of Korah", a family of Levites who served as temple singers. In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–21 are 49:1–20 in most English Bibles.
[^49-2]: Literally "both sons of *adam* and sons of *ish*", two words for "man", perhaps common people and people of rank.
[^49-3]: Hebrew *mashal*, a saying, parable or poem.
[^49-4]: Literally "the iniquity of my heels", probably those who follow at my heels. Or "my deceivers", a word from the same root (compare Genesis 27:36).
[^49-5]: Literally "A brother, a man surely cannot ransom".
[^49-6]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self. Also in verses 16 and 19.
[^49-7]: Literally "and it ceases forever". Or "and one must give it up forever".
[^49-8]: Hebrew *shachat*, "pit", a name for the grave and the realm of the dead.
[^49-9]: Literally "Their inward part: their houses forever". The Septuagint reads "Their graves are their homes forever", reading *qibram*, "their grave", for *qirbam*, "their inward part", with two letters reversed.
[^49-10]: Literally "does not spend the night". Verse 21, the refrain, reads "without understanding" (*velo yavin*) where this verse has "does not endure" (*bal yalin*).
[^49-11]: The Hebrew of this verse is difficult. Literally "This is their way, folly is theirs, and after them with their mouth they are pleased".
[^49-12]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning. Also in verse 16.
[^49-13]: Hebrew *Sheol*, the realm of the dead. Also in verse 16.
[^49-14]: The Hebrew text is written *tsiram*, perhaps "their image", but read *tsuram*, "their form".
[^49-15]: The Hebrew of the second half of this verse is difficult. Literally "and their form is for Sheol to wear out, from a dwelling for it".
[^49-16]: Literally "from the hand of Sheol".
[^49-17]: The same verb is used when God "took" Enoch (Genesis 5:24) and Elijah (2 Kings 2:3, 5). Compare 73:24.
[^49-18]: Literally "he blesses his soul (*nephesh*)".
[^49-19]: Or "you will go to the generation of your ancestors", or "it (his soul) will go".
[^49-20]: Compare verse 13.

## Chapter 50

*Status: draft*

**1** A psalm of Asaph.[^50-1] The Mighty One, God, Yahweh,[^50-2] has spoken and summoned the earth from the rising of the sun to its setting.

**2** Out of Zion, perfect in beauty, God shines out.

**3** Our God comes, and he will not keep silent;[^50-3] before him is a devouring fire, and around him a raging storm.

**4** He calls to the heavens above, and to the earth, to judge his people:

**5** "Gather to me my faithful ones,[^50-4] who made a covenant with me by sacrifice."

**6** The heavens proclaim his righteousness, for God himself is judge. Selah.[^50-5]

**7** "Listen, my people, and I will speak; Israel, I will testify against you: I am God, your God.

**8** It is not for your sacrifices that I rebuke you, or for your burnt offerings, which are always before me.

**9** I will not take a bull from your house, or goats from your pens.

**10** For every animal of the forest is mine, the cattle on a thousand hills.

**11** I know every bird of the mountains, and all that moves in the field is mine.[^50-6]

**12** If I were hungry, I would not tell you, for the world and all that fills it are mine.

**13** Do I eat the flesh of bulls, or drink the blood of goats?

**14** Offer God a sacrifice of thanksgiving,[^50-7] and fulfill your vows to the Most High.[^50-8]

**15** Call on me in the day of trouble; I will rescue you, and you will honor me."

**16** But to the wicked God says, "What right have you to recite my decrees, or to take my covenant on your lips?

**17** You hate discipline, and you throw my words behind you.

**18** When you see a thief, you are pleased with him,[^50-9] and you throw in your lot with adulterers.

**19** You let your mouth loose for evil, and your tongue harnesses deceit.

**20** You sit and speak against your brother; you slander your own mother's son.

**21** These things you have done, and I kept silent; you thought that I was just like you. But I will rebuke you, and lay out the case before your eyes.

**22** Understand this, you who forget God, or I will tear you to pieces, with no one to rescue you.

**23** Whoever offers a sacrifice of thanksgiving honors me; and to the one who keeps to the way,[^50-10] I will show the salvation of God."

[^50-1]: *Of Asaph*: or "for Asaph". Asaph was a Levite whom David appointed to lead the temple singers (1 Chronicles 16:5–7; 25:1–2). Psalms 73–83 are also "of Asaph". In the Hebrew text the title is part of verse 1. In this psalm it shares the verse with the first line, so the verse numbers match English Bibles.
[^50-2]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles. *The Mighty One, God, Yahweh*: Hebrew *El Elohim YHWH*, three names for God together, as in Joshua 22:22. It can also be read "The God of gods, Yahweh".
[^50-3]: Or "May our God come, and not keep silent".
[^50-4]: Hebrew *chasidim*, "the faithful, the loyal", related to *chesed*, "faithful love".
[^50-5]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning.
[^50-6]: Literally "is with me".
[^50-7]: Hebrew *todah*: a sacrifice given in thanksgiving (Leviticus 7:12). Also in verse 23.
[^50-8]: Hebrew *Elyon*, a title of God.
[^50-9]: Or "you run with him", as the Septuagint reads.
[^50-10]: Literally "and the one who sets a way". The meaning is uncertain.

## Chapter 51

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. A psalm of David,[^51-1]

**2** when the prophet Nathan came to him, after he had gone in to Bathsheba.[^51-2]

**3** Be gracious to me, God, according to your faithful love;[^51-3] according to your great compassion, blot out my transgressions.

**4** Wash me thoroughly from my iniquity, and cleanse me from my sin.

**5** For I know my transgressions, and my sin is always before me.

**6** Against you, you alone, have I sinned, and done what is evil in your sight, so that you are right when you speak, and blameless when you judge.[^51-4]

**7** Indeed, I was born in iniquity, and in sin my mother conceived me.

**8** Indeed, you desire truth in the inner being;[^51-5] in the hidden place you teach me wisdom.

**9** Purify me from sin with hyssop,[^51-6] and I will be clean; wash me, and I will be whiter than snow.

**10** Let me hear joy and gladness; let the bones you have crushed rejoice.

**11** Hide your face from my sins, and blot out all my iniquities.

**12** Create in me a clean heart, God, and renew a steadfast spirit within me.

**13** Do not cast me away from your presence, and do not take your holy spirit[^51-7] from me.

**14** Restore to me the joy of your salvation, and sustain me with a willing spirit.

**15** Then I will teach transgressors your ways, and sinners will return to you.

**16** Rescue me from bloodguilt,[^51-8] God, God of my salvation, and my tongue will sing aloud of your righteousness.

**17** Lord, open my lips, and my mouth will declare your praise.

**18** For you do not delight in sacrifice, or I would give it; you take no pleasure in burnt offering.

**19** The sacrifices of God are a broken spirit; a broken and crushed heart, God, you will not despise.

**20** Do good to Zion in your favor; build up the walls of Jerusalem.

**21** Then you will delight in right sacrifices,[^51-9] in burnt offerings and whole burnt offerings;[^51-10] then bulls will be offered on your altar.

[^51-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verses 1–2, so verses 3–21 are 51:1–19 in most English Bibles.
[^51-2]: *Gone in to*: had sexual relations with. The events are told in 2 Samuel 11–12.
[^51-3]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love.
[^51-4]: Romans 3:4 quotes the second half of this verse from the Septuagint: "so that you may be justified in your words, and prevail when you are judged".
[^51-5]: Hebrew *tuchot*, a rare word, perhaps "the inward parts" (compare Job 38:36).
[^51-6]: Literally "Un-sin me", the verb used of rites of purification. Hyssop, a small bushy plant, was used to sprinkle blood or water in such rites (Leviticus 14:4–7; Numbers 19:18).
[^51-7]: Literally "the spirit of your holiness". Compare Isaiah 63:10–11.
[^51-8]: Literally "from bloods", that is, from the guilt of shedding blood, or perhaps from a violent death.
[^51-9]: Literally "sacrifices of righteousness".
[^51-10]: Hebrew *kalil*, "whole": an offering burned up completely.

## Chapter 52

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. A maskil of David,[^52-1]

**2** when Doeg the Edomite came and told Saul, "David has come to the house of Ahimelech."[^52-2]

**3** Why do you boast of evil, you mighty man? The faithful love of God lasts all day long.[^52-3]

**4** Your tongue plots destruction like a sharpened razor, you worker of deceit.

**5** You love evil more than good, lying more than speaking righteousness. Selah.[^52-4]

**6** You love all words that devour, you deceitful tongue.

**7** But God will break you down forever; he will snatch you up and tear you from your tent; he will uproot you from the land of the living. Selah.

**8** The righteous will see and fear; they will laugh at him, saying,

**9** "See, this is the man who would not make God his stronghold, but trusted in his great wealth, and grew strong by his destruction."[^52-5]

**10** But I am like a green olive tree in the house of God; I trust in the faithful love of God forever and ever.

**11** I will give thanks to you forever, because you have done it. I will hope in your name, for it is good, in the presence of your faithful ones.[^52-6]

[^52-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Maskil*: a term of uncertain meaning, perhaps "a skillful psalm" or "a psalm of instruction". *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verses 1–2, so verses 3–11 are 52:1–9 in most English Bibles.
[^52-2]: The events are told in 1 Samuel 21:2–10 and 22:6–23 (21:1–9 in most English Bibles).
[^52-3]: Literally "The faithful love (*chesed*) of God all the day". The Septuagint reads "lawlessness all the day".
[^52-4]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning. Also in verse 7.
[^52-5]: Hebrew *havvah*, "destruction", as in verse 4, or "craving".
[^52-6]: Hebrew *chasidim*, "the faithful, the loyal", related to *chesed*, "faithful love".

## Chapter 53

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. According to Mahalath.[^53-1] A maskil of David.[^53-2]

**2** The fool[^53-3] says in his heart, "There is no God." They are corrupt; they do detestable wrong; there is no one who does good.

**3** God looks down from heaven on human beings, to see if there is anyone who understands, anyone who seeks God.

**4** They have all turned back;[^53-4] together they have become corrupt. There is no one who does good, not even one.[^53-5]

**5** Do they know nothing, the evildoers, who eat up my people as they eat bread, and do not call on God?

**6** There they are, in great terror, where there was nothing to fear! For God has scattered the bones of those who besieged you;[^53-6] you have put them to shame, for God has rejected them.[^53-7]

**7** Oh, that salvation for Israel would come from Zion! When God restores the fortunes of his people,[^53-8] let Jacob rejoice, let Israel be glad.

[^53-1]: Hebrew *al-machalat*, a term of uncertain meaning, perhaps a tune, or "for sickness" (from *chalah*, "be sick"). Also in the title of Psalm 88.
[^53-2]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Maskil*: a term of uncertain meaning, perhaps "a skillful psalm" or "a psalm of instruction". *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–7 are 53:1–6 in most English Bibles. Psalm 53 is almost the same as Psalm 14. It has *Elohim*, "God", where Psalm 14 has "Yahweh" (see the note on 42:2), and its verse 6 differs from 14:5–6.
[^53-3]: Hebrew *naval*, someone who is morally and spiritually senseless, not merely unintelligent.
[^53-4]: Hebrew *sag*. Psalm 14:3 has *sar*, "turned aside".
[^53-5]: Romans 3:10–12 quotes verses 2–4 freely (compare 14:1–3).
[^53-6]: Literally "the bones of the one who camps against you".
[^53-7]: Psalm 14:5–6 reads instead "There they are, in great terror, for God is with the generation of the righteous. You would frustrate the plans of the afflicted, but Yahweh is their refuge."
[^53-8]: Or "brings back his captive people".

## Chapter 54

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. With stringed instruments. A maskil of David,[^54-1]

**2** when the Ziphites came and said to Saul, "Is not David hiding among us?"[^54-2]

**3** God, save me by your name, and vindicate me by your might.

**4** God, hear my prayer; listen to the words of my mouth.

**5** For strangers[^54-3] have risen against me, and ruthless men seek my life;[^54-4] they do not set God before them. Selah.[^54-5]

**6** See, God is my helper; the Lord is the one who sustains my life.[^54-6]

**7** He will repay with evil those who lie in wait for me.[^54-7] In your faithfulness, destroy them.

**8** With a freewill offering I will sacrifice to you; I will give thanks to your name, Yahweh,[^54-8] for it is good.

**9** For he has rescued me from every trouble, and my eye has looked in triumph on my enemies.[^54-9]

[^54-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Maskil*: a term of uncertain meaning, perhaps "a skillful psalm" or "a psalm of instruction". *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verses 1–2, so verses 3–9 are 54:1–7 in most English Bibles.
[^54-2]: The events are told in 1 Samuel 23:19–28 and 26:1.
[^54-3]: Psalm 86:14, which is almost the same as this line, has "the arrogant" (*zedim*), a word that differs by one similar letter from *zarim*, "strangers".
[^54-4]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self.
[^54-5]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning.
[^54-6]: Literally "the Lord is among those who uphold my soul (*nephesh*)".
[^54-7]: The Hebrew text is written "Evil will return to those who lie in wait for me" but read "He will repay with evil those who lie in wait for me".
[^54-8]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^54-9]: Literally "my eye has looked on my enemies".

## Chapter 55

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. With stringed instruments. A maskil of David.[^55-1]

**2** Listen to my prayer, God, and do not hide yourself from my plea for mercy.

**3** Pay attention to me and answer me. I am restless in my complaint, and I groan,

**4** at the voice of the enemy, because of the pressure of the wicked. For they bring down trouble on me, and in anger they bear a grudge against me.

**5** My heart writhes within me; the terrors of death have fallen on me.

**6** Fear and trembling come upon me, and horror overwhelms me.

**7** And I say, "Oh, that I had wings like a dove! I would fly away and be at rest.

**8** See, I would flee far away; I would lodge in the wilderness. Selah.[^55-2]

**9** I would hurry to find a shelter from the raging wind and the storm."

**10** Confuse them, Lord, divide their tongues,[^55-3] for I see violence and strife in the city.

**11** Day and night they go around it on its walls; wickedness and trouble are within it.

**12** Destruction is within it; oppression and deceit never leave its square.

**13** For it is not an enemy who taunts me, or I could bear it; it is not one who hates me who exalts himself against me, or I could hide from him.

**14** But it is you, a man like myself,[^55-4] my companion and my close friend.

**15** Together we used to share sweet counsel; we walked with the throng in the house of God.

**16** Let death come on them by surprise;[^55-5] let them go down alive to Sheol,[^55-6] for evil is in their dwellings, in their midst.

**17** As for me, I call to God, and Yahweh[^55-7] will save me.

**18** Evening and morning and at noon I complain and groan, and he hears my voice.

**19** He redeems my life[^55-8] in safety from the battle against me, for many are against me.[^55-9]

**20** God will hear and answer them,[^55-10] he who is enthroned from of old. Selah. They never change their ways,[^55-11] and they do not fear God.

**21** He has stretched out his hands against his friends;[^55-12] he has violated his covenant.

**22** His speech is smoother than butter,[^55-13] but war is in his heart; his words are softer than oil, but they are drawn swords.

**23** Cast your burden[^55-14] on Yahweh, and he will sustain you; he will never let the righteous be shaken.

**24** But you, God, will bring them down to the pit of destruction;[^55-15] people of bloodshed and deceit will not live out half their days. But as for me, I will trust in you.

[^55-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Maskil*: a term of uncertain meaning, perhaps "a skillful psalm" or "a psalm of instruction". *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–24 are 55:1–23 in most English Bibles.
[^55-2]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning. Also in verse 20.
[^55-3]: Literally "Swallow up, Lord, divide their tongue". Compare the confusing of languages at Babel (Genesis 11:7–9).
[^55-4]: Literally "a man according to my rank", my equal.
[^55-5]: The Hebrew text is written as one word, *yeshimot*, "Desolations be on them!", but read as two, *yashi mavet*, "Let death come deceitfully on them".
[^55-6]: Hebrew *Sheol*, the realm of the dead. Compare Numbers 16:30–33.
[^55-7]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^55-8]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self.
[^55-9]: Literally "for with many they are with me": either many are against me, or many are on my side.
[^55-10]: That is, answer them with judgment. The same consonants can be read "humble them".
[^55-11]: Literally "who have no changes". The Hebrew of verses 20–22 is difficult in places.
[^55-12]: Literally "against those at peace with him".
[^55-13]: The Hebrew is unusual: literally "the butter-words of his mouth are smooth".
[^55-14]: Hebrew *yehav*, found only here, perhaps "what is given to you", your lot. The Septuagint reads "your care", and 1 Peter 5:7 echoes this line.
[^55-15]: Literally "the well of the pit".

## Chapter 56

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. According to "The Silent Dove of Far-off Places".[^56-1] Of David. A miktam,[^56-2] when the Philistines seized him in Gath.[^56-3]

**2** Be gracious to me, God, for a mere mortal hounds me; all day long an attacker oppresses me.

**3** Those who lie in wait for me hound me all day long, for many fight against me proudly.[^56-4]

**4** When I am afraid, I put my trust in you.

**5** In God, whose word I praise, in God I trust; I will not be afraid. What can flesh do to me?

**6** All day long they twist my words;[^56-5] all their thoughts are against me for evil.

**7** They gather together, they lie in hiding; they watch my steps,[^56-6] as they wait to take my life.[^56-7]

**8** Because of their wickedness, will they escape?[^56-8] In anger bring down the peoples, God.

**9** You have counted my wanderings;[^56-9] put my tears in your bottle.[^56-10] Are they not in your book?

**10** Then my enemies will turn back on the day I call. This I know: God is for me.

**11** In God, whose word I praise, in Yahweh,[^56-11] whose word I praise,

**12** in God I trust; I will not be afraid. What can a human being do to me?

**13** I am bound by vows to you, God;[^56-12] I will offer thanksgiving offerings to you.

**14** For you have rescued my life from death, and my feet from stumbling,[^56-13] so that I may walk before God in the light of life.[^56-14]

[^56-1]: Hebrew *al-yonat elem rechoqim*, probably the name of a tune: "the dove of silence of far-off places". The same consonants can be read "the dove of the distant terebinths".
[^56-2]: Hebrew *miktam*, a term of uncertain meaning (see the note on the title of Psalm 16).
[^56-3]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". The events are told in 1 Samuel 21:11–16 (21:10–15 in most English Bibles). In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–14 are 56:1–13 in most English Bibles.
[^56-4]: Hebrew *marom*, "on high": proudly, or perhaps addressed to God, "Most High".
[^56-5]: Or "they hurt my cause".
[^56-6]: Literally "my heels".
[^56-7]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self. Also in verse 14.
[^56-8]: The Hebrew of this line is difficult. Literally "On account of wickedness, deliver for them". Or "Because of their wickedness, cast them out".
[^56-9]: Or "my lament". Hebrew *nodi*, which sounds like *nod*, "bottle", in the next line.
[^56-10]: A skin bottle, for water, milk or wine.
[^56-11]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^56-12]: Literally "Upon me, God, are your vows", the vows I made to you.
[^56-13]: Literally "have you not rescued my feet from stumbling?"
[^56-14]: Or "in the light of the living".

## Chapter 57

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. According to "Do Not Destroy".[^57-1] Of David. A miktam,[^57-2] when he fled from Saul, in the cave.[^57-3]

**2** Be gracious to me, God, be gracious to me, for in you my soul[^57-4] takes refuge; in the shadow of your wings I will take refuge until the destruction passes by.

**3** I cry to God Most High,[^57-5] to God who fulfills his purpose for me.[^57-6]

**4** He will send from heaven and save me; he will put to shame the one who hounds me.[^57-7] Selah.[^57-8] God will send out his faithful love[^57-9] and his faithfulness.

**5** My soul is in the midst of lions; I lie down among those who breathe fire,[^57-10] human beings whose teeth are spears and arrows, whose tongue is a sharp sword.

**6** Be exalted above the heavens, God; let your glory be over all the earth.

**7** They set a net for my steps; my soul was bowed down. They dug a pit in my path; they have fallen into it themselves. Selah.

**8** My heart is steadfast, God, my heart is steadfast;[^57-11] I will sing and make music.

**9** Wake up, my glory![^57-12] Wake up, harp and lyre! I will wake the dawn.

**10** I will give thanks to you among the peoples, Lord; I will sing praise to you among the nations.

**11** For your faithful love is great, reaching to the heavens, and your faithfulness to the clouds.

**12** Be exalted above the heavens, God; let your glory be over all the earth.

[^57-1]: Hebrew *al-tashchet*, "do not destroy", probably the name of a tune (compare Isaiah 65:8). Also in the titles of Psalms 58, 59 and 75.
[^57-2]: Hebrew *miktam*, a term of uncertain meaning (see the note on the title of Psalm 16).
[^57-3]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". For the cave, compare 1 Samuel 22:1 and 24:4 (24:3 in most English Bibles). In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–12 are 57:1–11 in most English Bibles.
[^57-4]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self. Also in verses 5 and 7.
[^57-5]: Hebrew *Elyon*, a title of God.
[^57-6]: Literally "to God who completes for me". The Septuagint reads "who does good to me".
[^57-7]: Or "when the one who hounds me taunts me".
[^57-8]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning. Also in verse 7.
[^57-9]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love. Also in verse 11.
[^57-10]: Or "who devour".
[^57-11]: Verses 8–12 appear again, with small differences, as 108:2–6 (108:1–5 in most English Bibles).
[^57-12]: Hebrew *kevodi*, "my glory", perhaps meaning "my soul", the inner self (compare 16:9).

## Chapter 58

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. According to "Do Not Destroy".[^58-1] Of David. A miktam.[^58-2]
