**2** Do you indeed speak righteousness, you gods?[^58-3] Do you judge human beings fairly?

**3** No, in your hearts you devise wrongs; on the earth you weigh out the violence of your hands.

**4** The wicked go astray from the womb; liars wander off from birth.[^58-4]

**5** Their venom is like the venom of a snake, like a deaf cobra that stops up its ear,

**6** that will not listen to the voice of charmers, or of the most skillful caster of spells.

**7** God, break the teeth in their mouths; tear out the fangs of the young lions, Yahweh.[^58-5]

**8** Let them vanish like water that runs away; when he aims his arrows,[^58-6] let them be cut off.[^58-7]

**9** Let them be like a snail that melts away as it goes, like a woman's stillborn child, that never sees the sun.

**10** Before your pots can feel the heat of the thorns, whether green or burning, he will sweep them away.[^58-8]

**11** The righteous will rejoice when they see vengeance done; they will bathe their feet in the blood of the wicked.

**12** And people will say, "Surely there is a reward[^58-9] for the righteous; surely there is a God who judges on the earth."[^58-10]

[^58-1]: Hebrew *al-tashchet*, "do not destroy", probably the name of a tune (see the title of Psalm 57).
[^58-2]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". *Miktam*: a term of uncertain meaning (see the note on the title of Psalm 16). In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–12 are 58:1–11 in most English Bibles.
[^58-3]: The vowel points read *elem*, "silence": "Do you indeed speak righteousness in silence?" The same consonants can be read *elim*, "gods" or "mighty ones", the rulers addressed in this psalm (compare Psalm 82).
[^58-4]: Literally "from the belly".
[^58-5]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^58-6]: The Hebrew text is written "his arrow" but read "his arrows".
[^58-7]: The Hebrew of this line is difficult. Literally "he treads his arrows as they are cut off".
[^58-8]: The Hebrew of this verse is very difficult. Literally "Before your pots perceive the thornbush, like living, like burning, he will whirl it away".
[^58-9]: Literally "fruit".
[^58-10]: The verb "judges" is plural, as it sometimes is with *Elohim*, "God". Or "there are gods who judge".

## Chapter 59

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. According to "Do Not Destroy".[^59-1] Of David. A miktam, when Saul sent men, and they watched the house in order to kill him.[^59-2]

**2** Rescue me from my enemies, my God; protect me from those who rise up against me.

**3** Rescue me from evildoers, and save me from bloodthirsty men.[^59-3]

**4** For see, they lie in wait for my life;[^59-4] fierce men band together against me, not for any transgression or sin of mine, Yahweh.[^59-5]

**5** For no guilt of mine they run and take up their positions. Rouse yourself to meet me, and see!

**6** You, Yahweh God of Armies,[^59-6] God of Israel, wake up to punish all the nations; show no mercy to any treacherous evildoers. Selah.[^59-7]

**7** They return at evening, howling like dogs, and prowl around the city.

**8** See, they spew from their mouths; swords are on their lips, for they think, "Who will hear?"[^59-8]

**9** But you, Yahweh, laugh at them; you mock all the nations.

**10** Because of his strength, I will watch for you,[^59-9] for God is my fortress.

**11** My faithful God[^59-10] will come to meet me; God will let me look in triumph on those who lie in wait for me.

**12** Do not kill them, or my people will forget; make them wander by your power, and bring them down, Lord, our shield.

**13** For the sin of their mouths, the words of their lips, let them be caught in their pride, for the curses and the lies they speak.

**14** Consume them in wrath, consume them until they are no more, so that they may know that God rules over Jacob, to the ends of the earth. Selah.

**15** They return at evening, howling like dogs, and prowl around the city.

**16** They wander about for food, and if they are not satisfied, they stay all night.[^59-11]

**17** But I will sing of your strength; I will sing aloud of your faithful love in the morning. For you have been my fortress, a refuge in the day of my distress.

**18** My Strength, I will sing praises to you, for God is my fortress, my faithful God.[^59-12]

[^59-1]: Hebrew *al-tashchet*, "do not destroy", probably the name of a tune (see the title of Psalm 57).
[^59-2]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". *Miktam*: a term of uncertain meaning (see the note on the title of Psalm 16). The events are told in 1 Samuel 19:11–17. In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–18 are 59:1–17 in most English Bibles.
[^59-3]: Literally "men of blood".
[^59-4]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self.
[^59-5]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^59-6]: Hebrew *YHWH Elohim tseva'ot*. Compare "Yahweh of Armies", traditionally "the LORD of hosts" or "the LORD Almighty". *Tseva'ot* means armies: the heavenly hosts and the armies of Israel.
[^59-7]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning. Also in verse 14.
[^59-8]: Literally "for who hears?"
[^59-9]: Literally "His strength, I will watch for you", perhaps meaning the enemy's strength. Verse 18 has "my strength", and the Septuagint reads that here: "My strength, I will watch for you".
[^59-10]: Literally "The God of my faithful love (*chesed*)". The Hebrew text is written "The God of his faithful love" but read "The God of my faithful love".
[^59-11]: Or "they growl", "they grumble".
[^59-12]: Literally "the God of my faithful love (*chesed*)".

## Chapter 60

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. According to "The Lily of the Testimony".[^60-1] A miktam of David, for teaching,[^60-2]

**2** when he fought against Aram-naharaim and Aram-zobah,[^60-3] and when Joab returned and struck down twelve thousand of Edom in the Valley of Salt.[^60-4]

**3** God, you have rejected us and broken us; you have been angry. Restore us!

**4** You have made the land quake; you have split it open. Heal its cracks, for it is tottering.

**5** You have made your people see hard times; you have given us wine to drink that makes us stagger.

**6** You have set up a banner for those who fear you, for them to rally to, out of range of the bow.[^60-5] Selah.[^60-6]

**7** So that those you love may be rescued, save by your right hand, and answer me.[^60-7]

**8** God has spoken in his holiness:[^60-8] "I will exult; I will divide up Shechem, and measure out the Valley of Succoth.

**9** Gilead is mine, and Manasseh is mine; Ephraim is the helmet on my head;[^60-9] Judah is my scepter.[^60-10]

**10** Moab is my washbasin; on Edom I throw my sandal.[^60-11] Philistia, shout aloud because of me!"[^60-12]

**11** Who will bring me to the fortified city? Who will lead me to Edom?[^60-13]

**12** Is it not you, God, who have rejected us? You no longer go out, God, with our armies.

**13** Give us help against the adversary, for human help is worthless.[^60-14]

**14** With God we will do valiantly; he will trample our adversaries.

[^60-1]: Hebrew *al-shushan edut*, probably the name of a tune (compare the titles of Psalms 45, 69 and 80).
[^60-2]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Miktam*: a term of uncertain meaning (see the note on the title of Psalm 16). *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verses 1–2, so verses 3–14 are 60:1–12 in most English Bibles.
[^60-3]: *Aram-naharaim*, "Aram of the two rivers", was in northern Mesopotamia; *Aram-zobah* was an Aramean kingdom north of Damascus.
[^60-4]: The events are told in 2 Samuel 8:3–14 and 1 Chronicles 18:3–13. In 2 Samuel 8:13 David strikes down eighteen thousand of Aram in the Valley of Salt (the Septuagint and the Syriac read "Edom"); in 1 Chronicles 18:12 Abishai strikes down eighteen thousand Edomites there.
[^60-5]: Hebrew *qoshet*, probably a form of *qeshet*, "bow". Or "a banner to be raised because of the truth", taking *qoshet* as "truth", as in Aramaic.
[^60-6]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning.
[^60-7]: The Hebrew text is written "answer us" but read "answer me". Verses 7–14 appear again, with small differences, as 108:7–14 (108:6–13 in most English Bibles).
[^60-8]: Or "in his holy place".
[^60-9]: Literally "the stronghold of my head".
[^60-10]: Or "my ruler's staff" (compare Genesis 49:10).
[^60-11]: Throwing a sandal on land was perhaps a sign of taking possession of it (compare Ruth 4:7).
[^60-12]: Psalm 108:10 (108:9 in most English Bibles) has "over Philistia I shout in triumph".
[^60-13]: Literally "Who has led me to Edom?" Psalm 108:11 (108:10 in most English Bibles) has the same words; there "fortified city" is *ir mivtsar*, here *ir matsor*.
[^60-14]: Literally "the salvation of *adam* (humankind) is worthless".

## Chapter 61

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. With stringed instruments.[^61-1] Of David.[^61-2]

**2** Hear my cry, God; listen to my prayer.

**3** From the end of the earth I call to you, when my heart grows faint. Lead me to the rock that is higher than I.[^61-3]

**4** For you have been my refuge, a strong tower against the enemy.

**5** Let me live in your tent forever; let me take refuge in the shelter of your wings. Selah.[^61-4]

**6** For you, God, have heard my vows; you have given the inheritance of those who fear your name.

**7** Add days to the days of the king; may his years be like generation after generation.

**8** May he sit enthroned forever before God; appoint[^61-5] faithful love[^61-6] and truth to guard him.

**9** So I will sing praise to your name forever, as I fulfill my vows day after day.

[^61-1]: Hebrew *al-neginat*, "on a stringed instrument", a variant of *binginot*, "with stringed instruments", in the titles of Psalms 4, 6, 54 and 55.
[^61-2]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–9 are 61:1–8 in most English Bibles.
[^61-3]: Literally "On a rock too high for me, lead me". The Septuagint reads "You set me high on a rock".
[^61-4]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning.
[^61-5]: Hebrew *man*, an uncertain word, perhaps a short form of *mannah*, "appoint".
[^61-6]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love.

## Chapter 62

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. According to Jeduthun.[^62-1] A psalm of David.[^62-2]

**2** Truly[^62-3] my soul waits in silence for God;[^62-4] my salvation comes from him.

**3** Truly he is my rock and my salvation, my fortress; I will not be greatly shaken.

**4** How long will you all attack a man, to batter him down like a leaning wall, a tottering fence?[^62-5]

**5** Truly they plan to topple him from his high place; they delight in lies. With their mouths they bless, but inwardly they curse. Selah.[^62-6]

**6** Truly, my soul, wait in silence for God, for my hope comes from him.

**7** Truly he is my rock and my salvation, my fortress; I will not be shaken.

**8** My salvation and my glory rest on God;[^62-7] my strong rock, my refuge, is in God.

**9** Trust in him at all times, you people; pour out your hearts before him. God is a refuge for us. Selah.

**10** Truly, human beings are only a breath; people of rank are a lie.[^62-8] Placed on the scales, they go up; together they weigh less than a breath.

**11** Do not trust in extortion, or put empty hope in robbery; if wealth increases, do not set your heart on it.

**12** Once God has spoken; twice I have heard this: that power belongs to God,

**13** and faithful love[^62-9] belongs to you, Lord. For you repay each person according to what he has done.[^62-10]

[^62-1]: Hebrew *al-yedutun*. Jeduthun was one of David's chief musicians (1 Chronicles 16:41–42; 25:1–6); here perhaps the name of a tune or a style of music. Compare the titles of Psalms 39 and 77.
[^62-2]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–13 are 62:1–12 in most English Bibles.
[^62-3]: Hebrew *akh*, "truly" or "only", which begins verses 2, 3, 5, 6, 7 and 10.
[^62-4]: Literally "Truly to God my soul (*nephesh*) is silence". Also in verse 6.
[^62-5]: The Hebrew of this verse is difficult. Literally "How long will you rush upon a man? You will murder, all of you, like a leaning wall, a fence pushed in".
[^62-6]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning. Also in verse 9.
[^62-7]: Literally "On God my salvation and my glory".
[^62-8]: Literally "the sons of *adam* are a breath, the sons of *ish* are a lie", two words for "man", perhaps common people and people of rank (compare 49:3). *Breath*: Hebrew *hevel*, "vapor, breath", the word used throughout Ecclesiastes.
[^62-9]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love.
[^62-10]: Compare Job 34:11; Proverbs 24:12; Romans 2:6.

## Chapter 63

*Status: draft*

**1** A psalm of David, when he was in the wilderness of Judah.[^63-1]

**2** God, you are my God; I seek you earnestly.[^63-2] My soul[^63-3] thirsts for you; my body[^63-4] longs for you, in a dry and weary land without water.

**3** So I have gazed on you in the sanctuary, to see your power and your glory.

**4** Because your faithful love[^63-5] is better than life, my lips will praise you.

**5** So I will bless you as long as I live; in your name I will lift up my hands.

**6** My soul will be satisfied as with the richest food,[^63-6] and my mouth will praise you with joyful lips.

**7** When I remember you on my bed, I meditate on you through the watches of the night.

**8** For you have been my help, and in the shadow of your wings I sing for joy.

**9** My soul clings to you; your right hand holds me up.

**10** But those who seek my life to destroy it will go down into the depths of the earth.

**11** They will be handed over to the power of the sword;[^63-7] they will become food for jackals.[^63-8]

**12** But the king will rejoice in God; all who swear by him[^63-9] will boast, for the mouths of those who speak lies will be stopped.

[^63-1]: *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". Compare 1 Samuel 23:14 and 2 Samuel 15:23–28. In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–12 are 63:1–11 in most English Bibles.
[^63-2]: Hebrew *ashachareka*, related to *shachar*, "dawn"; so perhaps "I seek you at dawn".
[^63-3]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self. Also in verses 6, 9 and 10.
[^63-4]: Literally "my flesh".
[^63-5]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love.
[^63-6]: Literally "as with fat and fatness".
[^63-7]: Literally "They will pour him out on the hands of the sword".
[^63-8]: Or "foxes".
[^63-9]: By God, or perhaps by the king.

## Chapter 64

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. A psalm of David.[^64-1]

**2** Hear my voice, God, in my complaint; protect my life from the terror of the enemy.

**3** Hide me from the secret plots of evildoers, from the scheming throng of evildoers,[^64-2]

**4** who sharpen their tongues like swords, and aim bitter words like arrows,

**5** to shoot from hiding at the blameless; suddenly they shoot at him, and they have no fear.

**6** They encourage one another in an evil plan;[^64-3] they talk of hiding snares; they say, "Who will see them?"

**7** They devise injustices: "We have perfected a well-made plan!" For the inner thoughts and the heart of a man are deep.[^64-4]

**8** But God will shoot at them with an arrow; suddenly they will be wounded.

**9** They will be brought down, with their own tongues turned against them;[^64-5] all who see them will shake their heads.

**10** Then all people will fear; they will proclaim the work of God and ponder what he has done.

**11** Let the righteous rejoice in Yahweh[^64-6] and take refuge in him; let all the upright in heart give praise.[^64-7]

[^64-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–11 are 64:1–10 in most English Bibles.
[^64-2]: Two different Hebrew words, *mere'im* and *po'alei aven*, both "evildoers".
[^64-3]: Literally "They make strong for themselves an evil word".
[^64-4]: The Hebrew of this verse is difficult. Literally "They search out injustices; we have finished a searched-out search; and the inward part of a man and the heart are deep".
[^64-5]: Literally "They make him stumble, their tongue against them".
[^64-6]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^64-7]: Or "let them glory", "boast".

## Chapter 65

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. A psalm of David. A song.[^65-1]

**2** Praise waits for you in silence, God, in Zion,[^65-2] and to you vows will be fulfilled.

**3** You who hear prayer, to you all flesh will come.

**4** When iniquities[^65-3] overwhelm me, you atone for our transgressions.[^65-4]

**5** Happy[^65-5] is the one you choose and bring near to live in your courts! We will be satisfied with the good things of your house, your holy temple.

**6** With awesome deeds you answer us in righteousness, God of our salvation, the hope of all the ends of the earth and of the farthest seas,

**7** who sets the mountains firm by his strength, clothed with might,

**8** who stills the roaring of the seas, the roaring of their waves, and the tumult of the peoples.

**9** Those who live at the ends of the earth are in awe at your signs; you make the lands of the morning and the evening shout for joy.[^65-6]

**10** You care for the land and water it; you make it very rich. The stream of God is full of water; you provide their grain, for so you have prepared the land.[^65-7]

**11** You drench its furrows, you level its ridges; you soften it with showers and bless its growth.

**12** You crown the year with your goodness, and your tracks[^65-8] overflow with abundance.

**13** The pastures of the wilderness overflow; the hills are clothed with joy.[^65-9]

**14** The meadows are clothed with flocks, and the valleys are covered with grain; they shout for joy, yes, they sing.

[^65-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–14 are 65:1–13 in most English Bibles.
[^65-2]: Literally "To you silence (is) praise, God, in Zion". The Septuagint reads "Praise is fitting for you", taking the word *dumiyyah*, "silence", from a similar root.
[^65-3]: Literally "matters of iniquities".
[^65-4]: Or "you forgive", "you cover". Hebrew *kipper*, the verb used for making atonement.
[^65-5]: Hebrew *ashrei*: happy, fortunate; traditionally "blessed".
[^65-6]: Literally "you make the goings out of the morning and the evening shout for joy", that is, the far east and the far west.
[^65-7]: Literally "you have prepared it".
[^65-8]: Literally "your wagon tracks", as God's chariot passes over the land.
[^65-9]: Literally "gird themselves with joy".

## Chapter 66

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. A song. A psalm.[^66-1] Shout for joy to God, all the earth!

**2** Sing praises to the glory of his name; make his praise glorious.

**3** Say to God, "How awesome are your deeds! Because of your great power your enemies cringe before you.

**4** All the earth bows down to you and sings praises to you; they sing praises to your name." Selah.[^66-2]

**5** Come and see the works of God, awesome in his deeds toward human beings.

**6** He turned the sea into dry land; they passed through the river on foot. There we rejoiced in him.[^66-3]

**7** He rules by his might forever; his eyes keep watch on the nations. Let the rebellious not exalt themselves. Selah.

**8** Bless our God, you peoples; let the sound of his praise be heard,

**9** who has kept us among the living,[^66-4] and has not let our feet slip.

**10** For you have tested us, God; you have refined us as silver is refined.

**11** You brought us into the net;[^66-5] you laid a heavy burden on our backs.[^66-6]

**12** You let people ride over our heads; we went through fire and through water, but you brought us out to a place of abundance.[^66-7]

**13** I will come into your house with burnt offerings; I will fulfill my vows to you,

**14** the vows my lips uttered and my mouth spoke when I was in trouble.

**15** I will offer you burnt offerings of fat animals, with the smoke of rams; I will offer bulls with goats. Selah.

**16** Come and hear, all you who fear God, and I will tell what he has done for me.[^66-8]

**17** I cried aloud to him with my mouth, and his praise[^66-9] was on my tongue.

**18** If I had cherished wickedness in my heart, the Lord would not have listened.

**19** But truly God has listened; he has paid attention to the voice of my prayer.

**20** Blessed be God, who has not turned away my prayer, or his faithful love[^66-10] from me.

[^66-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. In the Hebrew text the title is part of verse 1. In this psalm it shares the verse with the first line, so the verse numbers match English Bibles.
[^66-2]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning. Also in verses 7 and 15.
[^66-3]: Or "There let us rejoice in him". Compare the crossing of the sea (Exodus 14) and of the Jordan (Joshua 3).
[^66-4]: Literally "who set our soul (*nephesh*) in life".
[^66-5]: Or "into prison", "into a stronghold".
[^66-6]: Literally "on our loins".
[^66-7]: Literally "to saturation". The Septuagint reads "to refreshment".
[^66-8]: Literally "for my soul (*nephesh*)".
[^66-9]: Literally "exaltation".
[^66-10]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love.

## Chapter 67

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. With stringed instruments. A psalm. A song.[^67-1]

**2** May God be gracious to us and bless us; may he make his face shine on us.[^67-2] Selah.[^67-3]

**3** May your way be known on earth, your salvation among all nations.[^67-4]

**4** Let the peoples praise you, God; let all the peoples praise you.

**5** Let the nations be glad and sing for joy, for you judge the peoples with fairness, and guide the nations on earth. Selah.

**6** Let the peoples praise you, God; let all the peoples praise you.

**7** The earth has yielded its harvest; may God, our God, bless us.

**8** May God bless us, and let all the ends of the earth fear him.

[^67-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–8 are 67:1–7 in most English Bibles.
[^67-2]: Literally "make his face shine with us". The verse echoes the priestly blessing in Numbers 6:24–26.
[^67-3]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning. Also in verse 5.
[^67-4]: Literally "to know on earth your way", that is, so that your way may be known.

## Chapter 68

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. Of David. A psalm. A song.[^68-1]

**2** Let God rise up, let his enemies be scattered; let those who hate him flee before him.[^68-2]

**3** As smoke is blown away, so blow them away; as wax melts before the fire, so let the wicked perish before God.

**4** But let the righteous be glad; let them rejoice before God; let them celebrate with joy.

**5** Sing to God, sing praises to his name; build up a road for the one who rides through the deserts.[^68-3] His name is Yah;[^68-4] rejoice before him.

**6** A father of the fatherless and a defender of widows is God in his holy dwelling.

**7** God gives the lonely a home to live in; he leads prisoners out into prosperity,[^68-5] but the rebellious live in a parched land.

**8** God, you went out before your people; you marched through the wasteland.[^68-6] Selah.[^68-7]

**9** The earth shook, the heavens poured down rain before God, the One of Sinai,[^68-8] before God, the God of Israel.

**10** You poured out abundant rain, God; when your inheritance was weary, you restored it.

**11** Your people[^68-9] settled in it; in your goodness, God, you provided for the afflicted.

**12** The Lord gives the word; the women who bring the good news are a great army:[^68-10]

**13** "The kings of armies flee, they flee!" And the woman who stays at home[^68-11] divides the plunder.

**14** Though you lie down among the sheepfolds,[^68-12] the wings of a dove are covered with silver, its feathers with shining gold.

**15** When the Almighty[^68-13] scattered kings there, it snowed on Zalmon.[^68-14]

**16** A mountain of God[^68-15] is the mountain of Bashan; a mountain of many peaks is the mountain of Bashan.

**17** Why do you look with envy,[^68-16] you mountains of many peaks, at the mountain God desired for his dwelling? Yes, Yahweh[^68-17] will dwell there forever.

**18** The chariots of God are twice ten thousand, thousands upon thousands; the Lord is among them, Sinai is in the holy place.[^68-18]

**19** You went up to the heights; you led captives away; you received gifts from people,[^68-19] even from the rebellious, so that Yah God might dwell there.

**20** Blessed be the Lord, who day after day bears our burden,[^68-20] the God who is our salvation. Selah.

**21** Our God is a God who saves,[^68-21] and to Yahweh the Lord belong the ways of escape from death.[^68-22]

**22** Surely God will crush the heads of his enemies, the hairy crown of the one who walks in his guilt.

**23** The Lord said, "I will bring them back from Bashan; I will bring them back from the depths of the sea,

**24** so that your foot may crush them in blood,[^68-23] and the tongues of your dogs may have their share of the enemies."[^68-24]

**25** Your processions have been seen, God, the processions of my God, my King, into the sanctuary.[^68-25]

**26** The singers go in front, the musicians last, and between them young women playing tambourines.

**27** In the assemblies bless God, Yahweh, you who come from the spring of Israel.[^68-26]

**28** There is little Benjamin, leading them,[^68-27] the princes of Judah in their throng,[^68-28] the princes of Zebulun, the princes of Naphtali.

**29** Your God has commanded your strength.[^68-29] Show your strength, God, you who have acted for us.

**30** Because of your temple at Jerusalem, kings will bring you gifts.

**31** Rebuke the beast among the reeds,[^68-30] the herd of bulls with the calves of the peoples, until it bows down with pieces of silver. He has scattered the peoples who delight in war.[^68-31]

**32** Envoys[^68-32] will come from Egypt; Cush[^68-33] will quickly stretch out its hands to God.

**33** Kingdoms of the earth, sing to God; sing praises to the Lord. Selah.

**34** To the one who rides in the heavens, the ancient heavens! Listen, he utters his voice, a mighty voice.

**35** Ascribe power to God, whose majesty is over Israel, and whose power is in the skies.

**36** You are awesome, God, from your sanctuary.[^68-34] The God of Israel is the one who gives strength and power to his people. Blessed be God!

[^68-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–36 are 68:1–35 in most English Bibles. This psalm is one of the most difficult in the Psalter. Many of its words are rare, some lines seem to be quotations from older poems, and the connection between its sections is often unclear. The notes mark the most uncertain lines.
[^68-2]: Compare Numbers 10:35, the words Moses spoke whenever the ark set out.
[^68-3]: Literally "cast up (a highway) for the one who rides in the *aravot*", the desert plains. Some take *aravot* as "clouds", comparing verse 34 and a title of the god Baal in Canaanite poetry, "rider of the clouds". The verb *sollu* may also mean "lift up (a song)".
[^68-4]: Hebrew *Yah*, a short form of the name Yahweh, as in *hallelu-yah*, "praise Yah". Also in verse 19.
[^68-5]: Hebrew *kosharot*, found only here, perhaps "prosperity" or "singing".
[^68-6]: Literally "God, when you went out before your people, when you marched through the wasteland", a sentence that continues in verse 9.
[^68-7]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning. Also in verses 20 and 33. Verses 8–9 are close to Judges 5:4–5.
[^68-8]: Literally "this Sinai", perhaps an old way of saying "the One of Sinai" (compare Judges 5:5).
[^68-9]: Literally "Your living things", perhaps "your flock" or "your community".
[^68-10]: Or "The Lord gives the word; great is the army of women who bring the news".
[^68-11]: Or "the beauty of the house". The Hebrew of verses 13–15 is very difficult, and their connection with the context is uncertain.
[^68-12]: Or "among the campfires", "between the saddlebags". The word is close to the one in Judges 5:16.
[^68-13]: Hebrew *Shaddai*. The meaning of the name is uncertain.
[^68-14]: A mountain, perhaps the one near Shechem (Judges 9:48), or one in Bashan. *Zalmon* is related to *tselem*, "shadow, darkness".
[^68-15]: Or "A mighty mountain", as "mountains of God" can mean mighty mountains (compare 36:7, English 36:6).
[^68-16]: Hebrew *teratsedun*, found only here; the meaning is uncertain.
[^68-17]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^68-18]: Literally "the Lord among them, Sinai, in holiness". Some scholars read "the Lord has come from Sinai into the holy place", with a small change to the Hebrew (compare Deuteronomy 33:2).
[^68-19]: Literally "among *adam* (humankind)". Ephesians 4:8 quotes this verse in a form that reads "he gave gifts to people".
[^68-20]: Or "who carries us".
[^68-21]: Literally "God is for us a God of acts of salvation".
[^68-22]: Literally "to death the goings out".
[^68-23]: Literally "so that you may crush your foot in blood". The Septuagint reads "so that your foot may be dipped in blood" (compare 58:11).
[^68-24]: Literally "the tongue of your dogs, from the enemies its portion".
[^68-25]: Or "in holiness".
[^68-26]: That is, you descendants of Israel.
[^68-27]: Or "ruling them".
[^68-28]: Hebrew *rigmatam*, found only here, perhaps "their crowd".
[^68-29]: The Septuagint reads "Command your strength, God".
[^68-30]: Probably Egypt, pictured as a crocodile or hippopotamus among the reeds of the Nile.
[^68-31]: The Hebrew of this verse is very difficult. Literally "...with calves of peoples, trampling himself with pieces of silver; he scattered peoples who delight in wars".
[^68-32]: Hebrew *chashmannim*, found only here; the meaning is uncertain, perhaps "envoys", "nobles" or "bronze".
[^68-33]: The lands on the upper Nile, south of Egypt.
[^68-34]: Literally "from your sanctuaries".

## Chapter 69

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. According to "Lilies".[^69-1] Of David.[^69-2]

**2** Save me, God, for the waters have come up to my neck.[^69-3]

**3** I sink in deep mire, where there is no foothold; I have come into deep waters, and the flood sweeps over me.

**4** I am worn out with my crying; my throat is parched. My eyes grow dim as I wait for my God.

**5** More than the hairs of my head are those who hate me without cause;[^69-4] mighty are those who would destroy me, my enemies without reason.[^69-5] What I did not steal, must I now give back?

**6** God, you know my folly; my guilty deeds are not hidden from you.

**7** Do not let those who hope in you be put to shame because of me, Lord Yahweh of Armies;[^69-6] do not let those who seek you be disgraced because of me, God of Israel.

**8** For it is for your sake that I have borne insults, that shame has covered my face.

**9** I have become a stranger to my brothers, a foreigner to my mother's sons.

**10** For zeal for your house has consumed me,[^69-7] and the insults of those who insult you have fallen on me.[^69-8]

**11** When I wept and humbled myself with fasting,[^69-9] it brought insults on me.

**12** When I made sackcloth my clothing, I became a byword to them.

**13** Those who sit at the gate talk about me, and drunkards make songs about me.[^69-10]

**14** But as for me, my prayer is to you, Yahweh, at a time of favor. God, in your great faithful love,[^69-11] answer me with your sure salvation.[^69-12]

**15** Rescue me from the mire, and do not let me sink; let me be rescued from those who hate me, and from the deep waters.

**16** Do not let the flood sweep over me, or the deep swallow me up, or the pit[^69-13] close its mouth over me.

**17** Answer me, Yahweh, for your faithful love is good; in your great compassion turn to me.

**18** Do not hide your face from your servant, for I am in distress; answer me quickly.

**19** Come near to me[^69-14] and redeem me; set me free because of my enemies.

**20** You know the insults I suffer, my shame and my disgrace; all my adversaries are before you.

**21** Insults have broken my heart, and I am sick.[^69-15] I looked for sympathy, but there was none, and for comforters, but I found none.

**22** They gave me poison[^69-16] for food, and for my thirst they gave me sour wine to drink.[^69-17]

**23** Let their table before them become a trap, and a snare for those at ease.[^69-18]

**24** Let their eyes be darkened so that they cannot see, and make their loins tremble continually.[^69-19]

**25** Pour out your fury on them, and let your burning anger overtake them.

**26** Let their camp be desolate; let no one live in their tents.[^69-20]

**27** For they persecute the one you have struck, and they talk about the pain of those you have wounded.[^69-21]

**28** Add guilt to their guilt; let them have no share in your righteousness.[^69-22]

**29** Let them be blotted out of the book of the living; let them not be enrolled[^69-23] with the righteous.

**30** But I am afflicted and in pain; let your salvation, God, set me safe on high.

**31** I will praise the name of God with a song, and magnify him with thanksgiving.

**32** This will please Yahweh more than an ox, a bull with its horns and hoofs.

**33** The humble will see it and be glad; you who seek God, let your hearts revive.

**34** For Yahweh hears the needy, and does not despise his own who are prisoners.

**35** Let heaven and earth praise him, the seas and everything that moves in them.

**36** For God will save Zion and rebuild the cities of Judah; they will live there and possess it.

**37** The descendants[^69-24] of his servants will inherit it, and those who love his name will live in it.

[^69-1]: Hebrew *al-shoshannim*, "according to lilies", probably the name of a tune (see the title of Psalm 45).
[^69-2]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–37 are 69:1–36 in most English Bibles.
[^69-3]: Hebrew *nephesh*, here "throat, neck". Elsewhere it means soul, life, self, as in verses 11 and 19.
[^69-4]: Compare John 15:25.
[^69-5]: Literally "my enemies of falsehood".
[^69-6]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles. *Yahweh of Armies* (Hebrew *YHWH tseva'ot*) is traditionally "the LORD of hosts" or "the LORD Almighty". *Tseva'ot* means armies: the heavenly hosts and the armies of Israel.
[^69-7]: Quoted in John 2:17.
[^69-8]: Quoted in Romans 15:3.
[^69-9]: Literally "When I wept in the fasting of my soul (*nephesh*)".
[^69-10]: Literally "and the songs of those who drink strong drink".
[^69-11]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love. Also in verse 17.
[^69-12]: Literally "in the faithfulness of your salvation".
[^69-13]: Literally "the well".
[^69-14]: Literally "to my soul (*nephesh*)".
[^69-15]: Hebrew *va'anushah*, found only here, perhaps "I am in despair".
[^69-16]: Hebrew *rosh*, a bitter and poisonous plant. The Septuagint reads "gall"; compare Matthew 27:34.
[^69-17]: Compare Matthew 27:48; Mark 15:36; John 19:28–29.
[^69-18]: Or "and for their allies". The Septuagint reads "and a retribution", and Romans 11:9–10 quotes verses 23–24 in that form.
[^69-19]: The Septuagint reads "and bend their backs continually", the wording quoted in Romans 11:10.
[^69-20]: Acts 1:20 applies this verse to Judas.
[^69-21]: The Septuagint reads "and they have added to the pain of those you have wounded".
[^69-22]: Literally "do not let them come into your righteousness".
[^69-23]: Literally "written".
[^69-24]: Literally "the seed".

## Chapter 70

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. Of David. To bring to remembrance.[^70-1]

**2** God, come to rescue me;[^70-2] Yahweh,[^70-3] come quickly to help me!

**3** Let those who seek my life[^70-4] be put to shame and confounded; let those who delight in my harm be turned back and disgraced.

**4** Let those who say, "Aha! Aha!" turn back because of their shame.[^70-5]

**5** But let all who seek you rejoice and be glad in you; let those who love your salvation say always, "Great is God!"[^70-6]

**6** As for me, I am afflicted and needy; God, come quickly to me! You are my help and my deliverer; Yahweh, do not delay.[^70-7]

[^70-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of David*: or "for David", "about David". *To bring to remembrance*: Hebrew *lehazkir* (see the note on the title of Psalm 38). In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–6 are 70:1–5 in most English Bibles. Psalm 70 is almost the same as 40:14–18 (40:13–17 in most English Bibles). In several places it has *Elohim*, "God", where Psalm 40 has "Yahweh" (see the note on 42:2), and the notes below give the other differences.
[^70-2]: Literally "God, to rescue me". Psalm 40:14 (40:13 in most English Bibles) has "Be pleased, Yahweh, to rescue me".
[^70-3]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^70-4]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self. Psalm 40:15 (40:14 in most English Bibles) adds "together" and "to sweep it away".
[^70-5]: Literally "turn back on the heel of their shame". Psalm 40:16 (40:15 in most English Bibles) has "Let them be appalled because of their shame", and "who say to me".
[^70-6]: Psalm 40:17 (40:16 in most English Bibles) has "Great is Yahweh!"
[^70-7]: Psalm 40:18 (40:17 in most English Bibles) has "may the Lord think of me" for "God, come quickly to me", and "my God, do not delay" for "Yahweh, do not delay".

## Chapter 71

*Status: draft*

**1** In you, Yahweh,[^71-1] I take refuge; let me never be put to shame.[^71-2]

**2** In your righteousness rescue me and deliver me; turn your ear to me and save me.

**3** Be a rock of refuge for me,[^71-3] where I can always come; you have given the command to save me, for you are my rock and my fortress.

**4** My God, rescue me from the hand of the wicked, from the grasp of the unjust and the ruthless.

**5** For you are my hope, Lord Yahweh, my trust from my youth.

**6** On you I have leaned from birth;[^71-4] you are the one who brought me out[^71-5] from my mother's womb. My praise is always of you.

**7** I have become like a portent to many, but you are my strong refuge.

**8** My mouth is filled with your praise, with your splendor all day long.

**9** Do not cast me off in the time of old age; do not abandon me when my strength fails.

**10** For my enemies speak against me; those who watch for my life[^71-6] plot together,

**11** saying, "God has abandoned him; pursue him and seize him, for there is no one to rescue him."

**12** God, do not be far from me; my God, hurry to help me!

**13** Let my accusers[^71-7] be put to shame and consumed; let those who seek my harm be covered with scorn and disgrace.

**14** But as for me, I will always hope, and I will add to all your praise.

**15** My mouth will tell of your righteousness, of your salvation all day long, though I do not know their number.[^71-8]

**16** I will come with the mighty deeds of the Lord Yahweh; I will make mention of your righteousness, yours alone.

**17** God, you have taught me from my youth, and to this day I declare your wonderful deeds.

**18** So even to old age and gray hairs, God, do not abandon me, until I declare your strength[^71-9] to the next generation, your might to all who are to come.

**19** Your righteousness, God, reaches to the heights, you who have done great things. God, who is like you?

**20** You who have made me see[^71-10] many troubles and calamities will give me life again; from the depths of the earth you will bring me up again.

**21** You will increase my greatness, and turn and comfort me.

**22** I too will praise you with the harp for your faithfulness, my God; I will sing praises to you with the lyre, Holy One of Israel.

**23** My lips will shout for joy when I sing praises to you, and so will my soul, which you have redeemed.

**24** My tongue too will speak of your righteousness all day long, for those who seek my harm have been put to shame and disgraced.

[^71-1]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^71-2]: Psalm 71 has no title. Verses 1–3 are close to 31:2–4 (31:1–3 in most English Bibles).
[^71-3]: Literally "a rock of dwelling". Psalm 31:3 (31:2 in most English Bibles) has "a rock of refuge (*ma'oz*), a house of fortresses".
[^71-4]: Literally "from the belly".
[^71-5]: Hebrew *gozi*, found only here; the meaning is uncertain, perhaps "the one who drew me out" or "my strength".
[^71-6]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self. Also in verses 13 and 23.
[^71-7]: Literally "the accusers of my soul (*nephesh*)". The word for "accusers" comes from the same root as *satan*, "adversary, accuser".
[^71-8]: Or "though I do not know how to count them".
[^71-9]: Literally "your arm".
[^71-10]: The Hebrew text is written "us" but read "me", both here and in "will give me life again".

## Chapter 72

*Status: draft*

**1** Of Solomon.[^72-1] God, give the king your justice,[^72-2] and the king's son your righteousness.

**2** May he judge your people with righteousness, and your afflicted ones with justice.

**3** May the mountains bring well-being[^72-3] to the people, and the hills, righteousness.

**4** May he defend the cause of the afflicted among the people, save the children of the needy, and crush the oppressor.

**5** May they fear you[^72-4] as long as the sun endures, and as long as the moon, through all generations.

**6** May he be like rain falling on mown grass, like showers that water the earth.

**7** In his days may the righteous flourish,[^72-5] and peace abound, until the moon is no more.

**8** May he rule from sea to sea, and from the River[^72-6] to the ends of the earth.

**9** May the desert tribes[^72-7] bow down before him, and his enemies lick the dust.

**10** May the kings of Tarshish and of the coastlands bring tribute; may the kings of Sheba and Seba[^72-8] offer gifts.

**11** May all kings bow down to him, and all nations serve him.

**12** For he rescues the needy who cry for help, and the afflicted who have no one to help them.

**13** He has pity on the poor and the needy, and saves the lives[^72-9] of the needy.

**14** From oppression and violence he redeems their life, and their blood is precious in his sight.

**15** May he live long! May gold of Sheba be given to him; may prayer be made for him continually, and may people bless him all day long.

**16** May there be abundant grain[^72-10] in the land, waving on the tops of the mountains; may its fruit be like Lebanon, and may people flourish in the cities[^72-11] like the grass of the field.

**17** May his name endure forever; may his name increase[^72-12] as long as the sun shines. May all nations be blessed in him; may they call him happy.[^72-13]

**18** Blessed be Yahweh[^72-14] God, the God of Israel, who alone does wonderful things.

**19** Blessed be his glorious name forever; may the whole earth be filled with his glory. Amen and Amen.[^72-15]

**20** The prayers of David son of Jesse are ended.[^72-16]

[^72-1]: Or "for Solomon", "about Solomon". Psalm 127 is the only other psalm with this title. In the Hebrew text the title is part of verse 1. In this psalm it shares the verse with the first line, so the verse numbers match English Bibles. The psalm is a prayer for the king. Later Jewish and Christian readers also heard in it a picture of the reign of the promised Messiah.
[^72-2]: Literally "your judgments".
[^72-3]: Hebrew *shalom*: peace, well-being, prosperity. Also in verse 7.
[^72-4]: The Septuagint reads "May he endure".
[^72-5]: The Septuagint reads "may righteousness flourish".
[^72-6]: That is, the Euphrates.
[^72-7]: Hebrew *tsiyyim*, "desert dwellers" or "desert creatures".
[^72-8]: Sheba was in southern Arabia; Seba was probably in Africa (compare Genesis 10:7; Isaiah 43:3).
[^72-9]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self. Also in verse 14.
[^72-10]: Hebrew *pissat bar*, found only here; perhaps "an abundance of grain".
[^72-11]: Literally "may they flourish from the city".
[^72-12]: Hebrew *yinnon*, found only here, perhaps "sprout", "increase".
[^72-13]: Compare God's promise to Abraham in Genesis 12:3; 22:18.
[^72-14]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^72-15]: Verses 18–19 are a doxology, a closing word of praise, which ends Book 2 of the Psalms (Psalms 42–72). Each of the five books ends with one (41:14, English 41:13; 72:18–19; 89:53, English 89:52; 106:48), and Psalm 150 closes the whole collection.
[^72-16]: This line probably closed an earlier collection of David's prayers. Several later psalms are also titled "of David" (for example Psalms 86, 101, 103, 108–110 and 138–145).

## Chapter 73

*Status: draft*

**1** A psalm of Asaph.[^73-1] Truly God is good to Israel, to those who are pure in heart.

**2** But as for me, my feet had almost stumbled; my steps had nearly slipped.

**3** For I envied the boastful, when I saw the prosperity[^73-2] of the wicked.

**4** For they have no pangs until their death;[^73-3] their bodies are healthy and well fed.

**5** They are not in trouble as others are;[^73-4] they are not struck as other people are.

**6** So pride is their necklace; violence covers them like a garment.

**7** Their eyes bulge from fatness;[^73-5] the imaginations of their hearts overflow.

**8** They mock and speak with malice; from on high they threaten oppression.

**9** They set their mouths against the heavens, and their tongues strut through the earth.

**10** So his people turn here,[^73-6] and drink up waters in abundance.[^73-7]

**11** And they say, "How does God know? Is there knowledge in the Most High?"[^73-8]

**12** See, these are the wicked; always at ease, they increase in wealth.

**13** Surely in vain I have kept my heart clean and washed my hands in innocence.

**14** For I have been struck all day long, and punished every morning.

**15** If I had said, "I will speak like this," I would have betrayed the generation of your children.

**16** But when I tried to understand this, it was troublesome in my eyes,

**17** until I went into the sanctuary of God;[^73-9] then I understood their end.

**18** Surely you set them on slippery ground; you make them fall into ruin.

**19** How suddenly they are destroyed, swept away completely by terrors!

**20** Like a dream when one awakes, Lord, when you rouse yourself,[^73-10] you will despise their image.[^73-11]

**21** When my heart was bitter, and I was pierced within,[^73-12]

**22** I was senseless and did not understand; I was like a beast[^73-13] before you.

**23** Yet I am always with you; you hold me by my right hand.

**24** You guide me with your counsel, and afterward you will take me into glory.[^73-14]

**25** Whom have I in heaven but you? And there is nothing on earth I desire besides you.[^73-15]

**26** My flesh and my heart may fail, but God is the rock[^73-16] of my heart and my portion forever.

**27** For see, those who are far from you will perish; you destroy all who are unfaithful to you.[^73-17]

**28** But as for me, it is good to be near God;[^73-18] I have made the Lord Yahweh[^73-19] my refuge, so that I may tell of all your works.

[^73-1]: *Of Asaph*: or "for Asaph", a Levite who led the temple singers (see the note on 50:1). Psalms 73–83 are all "of Asaph". In the Hebrew text the title is part of verse 1. In this psalm it shares the verse with the first line, so the verse numbers match English Bibles. Psalm 73 begins Book 3 of the Psalms (Psalms 73–89).
[^73-2]: Hebrew *shalom*: peace, well-being, prosperity.
[^73-3]: Literally "there are no pangs to their death". Some scholars divide the letters differently to read *lamo tam*, "they have no pangs; their bodies are sound", with a small change to the Hebrew.
[^73-4]: Literally "In the trouble of a mortal they are not".
[^73-5]: Literally "Their eye goes out from fat". The Septuagint reads "Their iniquity goes out from fat".
[^73-6]: That is, perhaps, to the wicked and their way of thinking. The Hebrew text is written "So he brings back his people here" but read "So his people turn here".
[^73-7]: The Hebrew of this verse is difficult. Literally "and waters of fullness are drained by them".
[^73-8]: Hebrew *Elyon*, a title of God.
[^73-9]: Literally "the sanctuaries of God".
[^73-10]: Or "in the city".
[^73-11]: Or "their phantom", "their shadow".
[^73-12]: Literally "in my kidneys". The kidneys were thought of as the seat of the emotions and the inner life.
[^73-13]: Hebrew *behemot*, "beasts", perhaps meaning "a great beast", "a brute".
[^73-14]: Or "and afterward you will receive me with honor", or "and lead me after glory". Compare 49:16.
[^73-15]: Literally "and with you I desire nothing on earth".
[^73-16]: Literally "the rock of my heart".
[^73-17]: Literally "everyone who goes away from you as a prostitute", a common picture of unfaithfulness to God.
[^73-18]: Literally "the nearness of God is good for me".
[^73-19]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.

## Chapter 74

*Status: draft*

**1** A maskil of Asaph.[^74-1] God, why have you rejected us forever? Why does your anger smolder against the sheep of your pasture?

**2** Remember your congregation, which you acquired long ago, the tribe of your inheritance, which you redeemed, Mount Zion, where you have dwelt.

**3** Turn your steps[^74-2] to the everlasting ruins; the enemy has damaged everything in the sanctuary.

**4** Your adversaries have roared in the midst of your meeting place;[^74-3] they have set up their own signs as signs.[^74-4]

**5** They looked like men raising axes in a thicket of trees.[^74-5]

**6** And now all its carved work they smash with hatchet and hammers.

**7** They set your sanctuary on fire; they have desecrated the dwelling place of your name, bringing it to the ground.

**8** They said in their hearts, "Let us crush them completely!"[^74-6] They burned all the meeting places of God in the land.

**9** We do not see our signs; there is no longer any prophet, and none of us knows how long.

**10** How long, God, will the adversary taunt? Will the enemy despise your name forever?

**11** Why do you hold back your hand, your right hand? Take it from the fold of your robe[^74-7] and destroy them!

**12** Yet God is my King from of old, working salvation in the midst of the earth.

**13** You split the sea by your strength; you broke the heads of the sea monsters[^74-8] on the waters.

**14** You crushed the heads of Leviathan;[^74-9] you gave him as food to the creatures of the desert.[^74-10]

**15** You split open springs and streams; you dried up ever-flowing rivers.

**16** Yours is the day, and yours also the night; you established the moon[^74-11] and the sun.

**17** You set all the boundaries of the earth; summer and winter, you made them.

**18** Remember this: the enemy taunts, Yahweh,[^74-12] and a foolish people despises your name.

**19** Do not give the life of your dove to the wild beasts;[^74-13] do not forget the life of your afflicted forever.

**20** Look to the covenant, for the dark places of the land are full of the haunts of violence.

**21** Do not let the oppressed turn back in shame; let the afflicted and the needy praise your name.

**22** Rise up, God, plead your own cause; remember how fools taunt you all day long.

**23** Do not forget the clamor of your adversaries, the uproar of those who rise against you, which goes up continually.

[^74-1]: *Maskil*: a term of uncertain meaning, perhaps "a skillful psalm" or "a psalm of instruction". *Of Asaph*: or "for Asaph", a Levite who led the temple singers (see the note on 50:1). In the Hebrew text the title is part of verse 1. In this psalm it shares the verse with the first line, so the verse numbers match English Bibles. The psalm mourns the destruction of the temple, probably by the Babylonians (2 Kings 25:8–10; compare Psalm 79).
[^74-2]: Literally "Lift up your steps".
[^74-3]: Hebrew *mo'ed*, "appointed place, meeting place", the word used in "tent of meeting".
[^74-4]: Perhaps military standards or the emblems of their gods.
[^74-5]: The Hebrew of this verse is difficult. Literally "It is known as one who brings up axes on high in a thicket of wood".
[^74-6]: Literally "Let us oppress them together".
[^74-7]: Literally "from the midst of your bosom". The Hebrew text is written "your decree" (*chuqqekha*) but read "your bosom" (*cheqekha*).
[^74-8]: Hebrew *tanninim*, great creatures of the sea (Genesis 1:21).
[^74-9]: A many-headed sea monster, a picture of the forces of chaos (compare Job 40:25, English 41:1; Isaiah 27:1).
[^74-10]: Literally "to a people, to desert creatures".
[^74-11]: Literally "the light".
[^74-12]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles. Or "the enemy taunts Yahweh".
[^74-13]: Literally "to the beast the soul (*nephesh*) of your turtledove". The Hebrew word *chayyat*, "beast", also means "life" or "community" in the next line.

## Chapter 75

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. According to "Do Not Destroy".[^75-1] A psalm of Asaph. A song.[^75-2]

**2** We give thanks to you, God, we give thanks, and your name is near; people tell of your wonderful deeds.

**3** "When I choose the appointed time,[^75-3] I myself will judge with fairness.

**4** When the earth and all who live in it melt away, it is I who hold its pillars firm. Selah.[^75-4]

**5** I said to the boastful, 'Do not boast,' and to the wicked, 'Do not lift up your horn.[^75-5]

**6** Do not lift up your horn on high, or speak with an arrogant neck.'"[^75-6]

**7** For not from the east or from the west, and not from the wilderness, comes lifting up.[^75-7]

**8** For God is the judge; he puts down one and lifts up another.

**9** For in the hand of Yahweh[^75-8] there is a cup, with foaming wine, richly mixed; he pours from it, and all the wicked of the earth must drain it to the dregs and drink.

**10** But as for me, I will declare it forever; I will sing praises to the God of Jacob.

**11** All the horns of the wicked I will cut off, but the horns of the righteous will be lifted up.[^75-9]

[^75-1]: Hebrew *al-tashchet*, "do not destroy", probably the name of a tune (see the title of Psalm 57).
[^75-2]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of Asaph*: or "for Asaph", a Levite who led the temple singers (see the note on 50:1). In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–11 are 75:1–10 in most English Bibles.
[^75-3]: God speaks in verses 3–6, or perhaps only in verses 3–4.
[^75-4]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning.
[^75-5]: The horn of an animal is a picture of strength and pride. Also in verses 6 and 11.
[^75-6]: Literally "with a neck of arrogance". The Septuagint reads "speak injustice against God".
[^75-7]: Hebrew *harim*, "lifting up", as in verses 5–6 and 8. Or "and not from the wilderness of the mountains".
[^75-8]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^75-9]: The speaker may be God, as in verses 3–6, or the psalmist.

## Chapter 76

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. With stringed instruments. A psalm of Asaph. A song.[^76-1]

**2** God is known in Judah; his name is great in Israel.

**3** His tent[^76-2] is in Salem,[^76-3] his dwelling place in Zion.

**4** There he broke the flaming arrows of the bow,[^76-4] the shield, the sword and the weapons of war.[^76-5] Selah.[^76-6]

**5** You are radiant, majestic, more than the mountains of prey.[^76-7]

**6** The stouthearted have been plundered; they have sunk into their sleep; none of the warriors could lift their hands.[^76-8]

**7** At your rebuke, God of Jacob, both chariot and horse lay stunned.[^76-9]

**8** You, you are awesome! Who can stand before you once your anger is roused?

**9** From heaven you pronounced judgment; the earth feared and was still,

**10** when God rose up to judge, to save all the humble of the earth. Selah.

**11** Surely human wrath will praise you; with the remnant of wrath you will clothe yourself.[^76-10]

**12** Make vows to Yahweh[^76-11] your God, and fulfill them; let all around him bring gifts to the one who is to be feared.

**13** He cuts short the spirit of princes; he is feared by the kings of the earth.

[^76-1]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of Asaph*: or "for Asaph", a Levite who led the temple singers (see the note on 50:1). In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–13 are 76:1–12 in most English Bibles.
[^76-2]: Or "his shelter", "his lair", as of a lion.
[^76-3]: Another name for Jerusalem (compare Genesis 14:18).
[^76-4]: Literally "the flames of the bow".
[^76-5]: Literally "and war".
[^76-6]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning. Also in verse 10.
[^76-7]: The Septuagint reads "from the everlasting mountains".
[^76-8]: Literally "did not find their hands".
[^76-9]: Literally "fell into a deep sleep".
[^76-10]: The Hebrew of this verse is difficult. Literally "the remnant of wraths you will gird on". Or "the survivors of wrath will keep your festivals".
[^76-11]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.

## Chapter 77

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. According to Jeduthun.[^77-1] Of Asaph. A psalm.[^77-2]

**2** My voice goes up to God, and I cry out; my voice goes up to God, and he will listen to me.[^77-3]

**3** In the day of my trouble I seek the Lord; at night my hand is stretched out without growing weary;[^77-4] my soul[^77-5] refuses to be comforted.

**4** I remember God, and I groan; I meditate, and my spirit grows faint. Selah.[^77-6]

**5** You hold my eyelids open;[^77-7] I am so troubled that I cannot speak.

**6** I think about the days of old, the years long past.

**7** I remember my song in the night; I meditate in my heart, and my spirit searches:

**8** "Will the Lord reject forever? Will he never again show favor?

**9** Has his faithful love[^77-8] ceased forever? Has his promise[^77-9] come to an end for all generations?

**10** Has God forgotten to be gracious? Has he in anger shut up his compassion?" Selah.

**11** Then I said, "This is my grief: that the right hand of the Most High has changed."[^77-10]

**12** I will remember[^77-11] the deeds of Yah;[^77-12] yes, I will remember your wonders of old.

**13** I will meditate on all your work, and ponder your mighty deeds.

**14** God, your way is in holiness.[^77-13] What god is great like God?

**15** You are the God who works wonders; you have made known your strength among the peoples.

**16** With your arm you redeemed your people, the descendants of Jacob and Joseph. Selah.

**17** The waters saw you, God, the waters saw you and writhed; the depths too trembled.

**18** The clouds poured down water; the skies thundered;[^77-14] your arrows flashed back and forth.

**19** The sound of your thunder was in the whirlwind;[^77-15] lightning lit up the world; the earth trembled and shook.

**20** Your way was through the sea, your path[^77-16] through the mighty waters, yet your footprints were not seen.

**21** You led your people like a flock by the hand of Moses and Aaron.

[^77-1]: Hebrew *al-yedutun*. The Hebrew text is written "Jedithun" but read "Jeduthun". Jeduthun was one of David's chief musicians (see the note on the title of Psalm 62).
[^77-2]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of Asaph*: or "for Asaph", a Levite who led the temple singers (see the note on 50:1). In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–21 are 77:1–20 in most English Bibles.
[^77-3]: Literally "My voice to God, and I cry out; my voice to God, and listen to me".
[^77-4]: Literally "my hand at night was poured out and did not grow numb", that is, stretched out in prayer.
[^77-5]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self.
[^77-6]: Hebrew *selah*, a musical or liturgical term of uncertain meaning. Also in verses 10 and 16.
[^77-7]: Literally "You have held the guards of my eyes".
[^77-8]: Hebrew *chesed*: loyal, faithful love.
[^77-9]: Literally "word".
[^77-10]: The Hebrew of this verse is difficult. Literally "My sickness, it is the changing of the right hand of the Most High". *Shenot*, "changing", can also mean "years": "the years of the right hand of the Most High". *Most High*: Hebrew *Elyon*, a title of God.
[^77-11]: The Hebrew text is written "I will make mention of" but read "I will remember".
[^77-12]: Hebrew *Yah*, a short form of the name Yahweh, as in *hallelu-yah*, "praise Yah".
[^77-13]: Or "in the holy place".
[^77-14]: Literally "gave a voice".
[^77-15]: Hebrew *galgal*, "wheel" or "whirlwind".
[^77-16]: The Hebrew text is written "your paths" but read "your path".

## Chapter 78

*Status: draft*

**1** A maskil of Asaph.[^78-1] Listen, my people, to my instruction;[^78-2] turn your ears to the words of my mouth.

**2** I will open my mouth in a parable;[^78-3] I will pour out riddles from of old,[^78-4]

**3** things that we have heard and known, that our ancestors have told us.

**4** We will not hide them from their children; we will tell the next generation the praiseworthy deeds of Yahweh,[^78-5] his might, and the wonders he has done.

**5** He established a statute[^78-6] in Jacob, and set a law in Israel, which he commanded our ancestors to make known to their children,

**6** so that the next generation might know them, the children yet to be born, and they in turn would rise up and tell their children,

**7** so that they would put their confidence in God, and not forget the works of God, but keep his commands,

**8** and so that they would not be like their ancestors, a stubborn and rebellious generation, a generation whose heart was not steadfast, whose spirit was not faithful to God.

**9** The Ephraimites, armed with bows,[^78-7] turned back on the day of battle.

**10** They did not keep God's covenant, and they refused to walk in his law.

**11** They forgot what he had done, the wonders he had shown them.

**12** In the sight of their ancestors he did wonders in the land of Egypt, in the region of Zoan.[^78-8]

**13** He split the sea and led them through; he made the waters stand up like a heap.[^78-9]

**14** He led them with the cloud by day, and all night long with the light of fire.

**15** He split rocks in the wilderness, and gave them drink as abundant as the great deep.[^78-10]

**16** He brought streams out of the rock, and made water flow down like rivers.

**17** But they went on sinning against him, rebelling against the Most High[^78-11] in the desert.

**18** They put God to the test in their hearts, demanding food for their craving.[^78-12]

**19** They spoke against God; they said, "Can God spread a table in the wilderness?

**20** True, he struck the rock, and water gushed out, and streams overflowed. But can he also give bread, or provide meat for his people?"

**21** So when Yahweh heard, he was furious; a fire was kindled against Jacob, and his anger rose against Israel,[^78-13]

**22** because they did not believe in God, or trust in his salvation.

**23** Yet he commanded the skies above, and opened the doors of heaven;

**24** he rained down manna on them to eat, and gave them the grain of heaven.[^78-14]

**25** Each of them ate the bread of the mighty;[^78-15] he sent them food in abundance.

**26** He let loose the east wind in the heavens, and by his power he drove out the south wind.

**27** He rained meat on them like dust, winged birds like the sand of the seas.

**28** He let them fall in the middle of their camp, all around their dwellings.[^78-16]

**29** So they ate and were well filled, for he gave them what they craved.

**30** But before they had turned from their craving, while the food was still in their mouths,

**31** the anger of God rose against them; he killed the strongest of them,[^78-17] and struck down the young men of Israel.[^78-18]

**32** In spite of all this, they went on sinning; they did not believe in his wonders.

**33** So he made their days vanish like a breath,[^78-19] and their years in sudden terror.

**34** Whenever he killed some of them, the rest would seek him; they would turn and look eagerly for God.

**35** They remembered that God was their rock, God Most High their redeemer.

**36** But they flattered him with their mouths, and lied to him with their tongues.

**37** Their hearts were not steadfast toward him; they were not faithful to his covenant.

**38** Yet he, being compassionate, forgave[^78-20] their iniquity and did not destroy them; again and again he held back his anger, and did not stir up all his wrath.

**39** He remembered that they were only flesh, a breath of wind[^78-21] that passes and does not return.

**40** How often they rebelled against him in the wilderness, and grieved him in the wasteland!

**41** Again and again they put God to the test, and provoked[^78-22] the Holy One of Israel.

**42** They did not remember his power,[^78-23] the day when he redeemed them from the adversary,

**43** when he displayed his signs in Egypt, and his wonders in the region of Zoan.[^78-24]

**44** He turned their rivers[^78-25] into blood, so that they could not drink from their streams.

**45** He sent swarms of flies among them, which devoured them, and frogs, which destroyed them.

**46** He gave their crops to the grasshopper, and the fruit of their labor to the locust.

**47** He killed their vines with hail, and their sycamore-fig trees with frost.[^78-26]

**48** He gave over their cattle to the hail, and their livestock to bolts of lightning.

**49** He let loose on them his burning anger, fury, indignation and distress, a band of destroying angels.[^78-27]

**50** He cleared a path for his anger; he did not spare them from death, but gave their lives[^78-28] over to the plague.

**51** He struck down every firstborn in Egypt, the firstfruits of their strength in the tents of Ham.[^78-29]

**52** Then he led out his people like sheep, and guided them like a flock in the wilderness.

**53** He led them in safety, so that they were not afraid; but the sea covered their enemies.

**54** He brought them to his holy land,[^78-30] to this mountain that his right hand had won.

**55** He drove out nations before them, and allotted their land as an inheritance by measuring line;[^78-31] he settled the tribes of Israel in their tents.

**56** Yet they tested God Most High and rebelled against him, and did not keep his statutes.

**57** They turned away and were treacherous like their ancestors; they twisted like a faulty bow.[^78-32]

**58** They provoked him to anger with their high places, and made him jealous with their carved images.

**59** When God heard, he was furious, and he utterly rejected Israel.

**60** He abandoned the tabernacle at Shiloh, the tent where he had dwelt among human beings.[^78-33]

**61** He gave up his strength to captivity, his splendor into the hand of the adversary.[^78-34]

**62** He handed his people over to the sword, and he was furious with his inheritance.

**63** Fire consumed their young men, and their young women had no wedding songs.[^78-35]

**64** Their priests fell by the sword, and their widows could not weep.

**65** Then the Lord awoke as from sleep, like a warrior shouting because of wine.[^78-36]

**66** He beat back his adversaries;[^78-37] he put them to everlasting shame.

**67** He rejected the tent of Joseph, and did not choose the tribe of Ephraim;

**68** but he chose the tribe of Judah, Mount Zion, which he loves.

**69** He built his sanctuary like the heights,[^78-38] like the earth, which he founded forever.

**70** He chose David his servant, and took him from the sheepfolds;

**71** from following the nursing ewes he brought him to shepherd Jacob his people, Israel his inheritance.

**72** He shepherded them with integrity of heart,[^78-39] and guided them with skillful hands.

[^78-1]: *Maskil*: a term of uncertain meaning, perhaps "a skillful psalm" or "a psalm of instruction". *Of Asaph*: or "for Asaph", a Levite who led the temple singers (see the note on 50:1). In the Hebrew text the title is part of verse 1. In this psalm it shares the verse with the first line, so the verse numbers match English Bibles. The psalm retells Israel's history from the exodus to the choice of David and Zion, as a warning to each new generation.
[^78-2]: Hebrew *torah*, "instruction, teaching".
[^78-3]: Hebrew *mashal*, a saying, parable or poem.
[^78-4]: Matthew 13:35 quotes this verse.
[^78-5]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^78-6]: Hebrew *edut*, traditionally "testimony": the solemn terms of the covenant. Also in verse 56, in the plural.
[^78-7]: Literally "equipped shooters of the bow". The event is not known.
[^78-8]: Literally "the field of Zoan". Zoan was a city in the eastern Nile delta (Numbers 13:22).
[^78-9]: Compare Exodus 14:21–22; 15:8.
[^78-10]: Literally "like the great deeps". Compare Exodus 17:6; Numbers 20:11.
[^78-11]: Hebrew *Elyon*, a title of God. Also in verses 35 and 56.
[^78-12]: Literally "for their soul (*nephesh*)", that is, their appetite.
[^78-13]: Verses 18–31 recall Exodus 16 and Numbers 11.
[^78-14]: John 6:31 quotes this verse.
[^78-15]: Hebrew *abbirim*, "mighty ones". The Septuagint reads "the bread of angels".
[^78-16]: Literally "his camp... his dwellings": Israel's.
[^78-17]: Literally "among their fat ones".
[^78-18]: Compare Numbers 11:33–34.
[^78-19]: Hebrew *hevel*, "vapor, breath".
[^78-20]: Or "atoned for", "covered". Hebrew *kipper*, the verb used for making atonement.
[^78-21]: Hebrew *ruach*, "wind, breath, spirit".
[^78-22]: Or "pained". The meaning of the Hebrew *hitvu* is uncertain.
[^78-23]: Literally "his hand".
[^78-24]: Verses 44–51 recall the plagues of Egypt (Exodus 7–12), not all of them, and not in the same order.
[^78-25]: Hebrew *ye'orim*, the Nile and its channels.
[^78-26]: Hebrew *chanamal*, found only here, perhaps "frost" or "hailstones".
[^78-27]: Literally "a sending of messengers of evils".
[^78-28]: Hebrew *nephesh*: soul, life, self. The second line can also be read "but gave their animals over to the plague".
[^78-29]: Ham was the ancestor of the Egyptians (Genesis 10:6).
[^78-30]: Literally "to the territory of his holiness".
[^78-31]: Literally "and made them fall by a line as an inheritance".
[^78-32]: Literally "a bow of deceit", one that does not shoot straight.
[^78-33]: See 1 Samuel 4 and Jeremiah 7:12–14.
[^78-34]: That is, the ark of the covenant, captured by the Philistines (1 Samuel 4:21–22).
[^78-35]: Literally "were not praised".
[^78-36]: Or "like a warrior overcome by wine". The meaning of *mitronen* is uncertain.
[^78-37]: Or "He struck his adversaries from behind".
[^78-38]: Literally "like high places", perhaps the heavens.
[^78-39]: Literally "according to the blamelessness of his heart".

## Chapter 79

*Status: draft*

**1** A psalm of Asaph.[^79-1] God, the nations have come into your inheritance; they have defiled your holy temple; they have made Jerusalem a heap of ruins.[^79-2]

**2** They have given the corpses of your servants as food to the birds of the sky, the flesh of your faithful ones[^79-3] to the wild animals of the earth.

**3** They have poured out their blood like water all around Jerusalem, and there is no one to bury them.[^79-4]

**4** We have become a disgrace to our neighbors, the scorn and mockery of those around us.

**5** How long, Yahweh?[^79-5] Will you be angry forever? Will your jealousy[^79-6] burn like fire?

**6** Pour out your wrath on the nations that do not know you, and on the kingdoms that do not call on your name.

**7** For they have devoured Jacob,[^79-7] and laid waste his homeland.[^79-8]

**8** Do not hold against us the iniquities of former times;[^79-9] let your compassion come quickly to meet us, for we have been brought very low.

**9** Help us, God of our salvation, for the sake of the glory of your name; rescue us and atone for our sins,[^79-10] for the sake of your name.

**10** Why should the nations say, "Where is their God?" Before our eyes, let it be known among the nations that you avenge the blood of your servants that has been poured out.[^79-11]

**11** Let the groaning of the prisoner come before you; by the greatness of your arm, preserve[^79-12] those condemned to die.[^79-13]

**12** Pay back our neighbors sevenfold into their laps[^79-14] for the taunts with which they have taunted you, Lord.

**13** Then we, your people, the sheep of your pasture, will give thanks to you forever; from generation to generation we will tell of your praise.

[^79-1]: *Of Asaph*: or "for Asaph", a Levite who led the temple singers (see the note on 50:1). In the Hebrew text the title is part of verse 1. In this psalm it shares the verse with the first line, so the verse numbers match English Bibles. The psalm mourns the plundering of Jerusalem and its temple, probably by the Babylonians (2 Kings 25:8–10; compare Psalm 74).
[^79-2]: Compare Micah 3:12 and Jeremiah 26:18.
[^79-3]: Hebrew *chasidim*, "the faithful, the loyal", related to *chesed*, "faithful love".
[^79-4]: 1 Maccabees 7:17 quotes verses 2–3.
[^79-5]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles.
[^79-6]: Or "zeal".
[^79-7]: The verb is singular in the Hebrew, "he has devoured", that is, the enemy. Verses 6–7 are nearly the same as Jeremiah 10:25.
[^79-8]: Or "his pasture", "his dwelling place".
[^79-9]: Literally "the iniquities of the first ones": either our own former sins, or the sins of our ancestors.
[^79-10]: Or "cover our sins", "forgive our sins". Hebrew *kapper*, the verb used for making atonement.
[^79-11]: Literally "Let the avenging of the poured-out blood of your servants be known among the nations before our eyes."
[^79-12]: Literally "leave over", that is, keep alive.
[^79-13]: Literally "the sons of death".
[^79-14]: Literally "into their bosom", the fold of a garment above the belt, where things were carried.

## Chapter 80

*Status: draft*

**1** For the director. According to "Lilies".[^80-1] A testimony[^80-2] of Asaph. A psalm.[^80-3]

**2** Shepherd of Israel, listen, you who lead Joseph like a flock! You who are enthroned on the cherubim,[^80-4] shine out!

**3** Before Ephraim, Benjamin and Manasseh, stir up your might, and come to save us.

**4** God, restore us;[^80-5] let your face shine, and we will be saved.[^80-6]

**5** Yahweh God of Armies,[^80-7] how long will you smolder with anger[^80-8] against the prayer of your people?

**6** You have fed them the bread of tears, and made them drink tears by the measure.[^80-9]

**7** You make us an object of strife for our neighbors, and our enemies laugh among themselves.[^80-10]

**8** God of Armies, restore us; let your face shine, and we will be saved.

**9** You brought a vine out of Egypt; you drove out nations and planted it.

**10** You cleared the ground before it; it took deep root and filled the land.

**11** The mountains were covered with its shade, and the mighty cedars[^80-11] with its branches.

**12** It sent out its boughs to the Sea, and its shoots to the River.[^80-12]

**13** Why have you broken down its walls, so that all who pass along the road pluck its fruit?

**14** The boar from the forest[^80-13] ravages it, and all that moves in the field feeds on it.

**15** God of Armies, turn back, please! Look down from heaven and see; take care of this vine,

**16** the stock[^80-14] that your right hand planted, and the son[^80-15] you made strong for yourself.

**17** It is burned with fire; it is cut down. At the rebuke of your face they perish.

**18** Let your hand be on the man of your right hand,[^80-16] on the son of man[^80-17] you made strong for yourself.

**19** Then we will not turn away from you; give us life, and we will call on your name.

**20** Yahweh God of Armies, restore us; let your face shine, and we will be saved.

[^80-1]: Hebrew *el-shoshannim*, "according to lilies", probably the name of a tune (compare the titles of Psalms 45 and 69).
[^80-2]: Hebrew *edut*, "testimony", perhaps part of the name of the tune, "Lilies of the Testimony" (compare the title of Psalm 60).
[^80-3]: *For the director*: Hebrew *lamenatseach*, probably the leader of the temple music. *Of Asaph*: or "for Asaph", a Levite who led the temple singers (see the note on 50:1). In the Hebrew text the title is verse 1, so verses 2–20 are 80:1–19 in most English Bibles.
[^80-4]: Or "above the cherubim". Hebrew *keruvim*: winged heavenly beings. Two gold cherubim stood on the cover of the ark (Exodus 25:18–22).
[^80-5]: Or "bring us back". The refrain comes again, growing longer each time, in verses 8 and 20.
[^80-6]: *Let your face shine*: compare the priestly blessing in Numbers 6:25.
[^80-7]: *Yahweh*: God's personal name (Hebrew YHWH), traditionally rendered "the LORD" in English Bibles. *Yahweh God of Armies*: Hebrew *YHWH Elohim tseva'ot*. Compare "Yahweh of Armies", traditionally "the LORD of hosts" or "the LORD Almighty". *Tseva'ot* means armies: the heavenly hosts and the armies of Israel. Verse 20 has the same title, and verses 8 and 15 have *Elohim tseva'ot*, "God of Armies".
[^80-8]: Literally "will you smoke".
[^80-9]: Hebrew *shalish*, "a third", a measure of uncertain size, probably large.
[^80-10]: Literally "mock for themselves". The Septuagint reads "mock us".
[^80-11]: Literally "the cedars of God", that is, the tallest cedars.
[^80-12]: The Mediterranean Sea and the river Euphrates, the ideal limits of the land (compare Exodus 23:31).
[^80-13]: In the Hebrew text the letter *ayin* of *miya'ar*, "from the forest", is written raised above the line, a scribal mark. According to Jewish tradition it marks the middle letter of the book of Psalms.
[^80-14]: Hebrew *kannah*, found only here, probably "stock" or "root". Some take it as a verb, "protect".
[^80-15]: Hebrew *ben*, "son", here also a branch of the vine (compare Genesis 49:22, where Joseph is a fruitful *ben*). The Septuagint reads "the son of man", as in verse 18.
